ParlaMint-ES_2015-09-16-CD150916.u1	The meeting was resumed. We continue with the debate on the opinion of the General State Budgets Bill for 2016. We will discuss section 18, Education, Culture and Sport. For the section presentation, I have the floor on behalf of the Government, the Minister for Education, Culture and Sport.
ParlaMint-ES_2015-09-16-CD150916.u2	Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, it is a pleasure for me to appear before your ladies and gentlemen to present to you the general outlines of Section 18 of the General Budgets of the State, on which the work agenda of the Ministry of Education, Culture and Sport, which I have the honour to address, is based. The ministry's budgets in 2016 are the result of a change in our country's economic scenario. The structural reforms carried out by the Government under the principles of efficiency and accountability coupled with the efforts of all citizens have placed us on the path of economic growth for several quarters. These 2016 budgets are not only an exercise of efficiency, effectiveness, prioritization of spending and political rationality, but also the budgets of recovery, which now allow us to push and reinforce a whole series of budget lines on a basis that has remained unchanged throughout the parliamentary term. Firstly, education policy is a fundamental pillar of the social action of the Government and of our commitment to equal opportunities. The main objective of our education system is to develop the potential of each student and his/her acquisition of skills. It is our duty to work for an educational system capable of meeting citizens' expectations and in which the complementarity between training and the labour market becomes a reality. A system in which we must pay attention to merit, effort and capacity within a framework of certainty, quality and freedom without exclusion of any kind. These budgets are a firm commitment, on the one hand, for equity and equal opportunities to become a real mechanism for mobility and social promotion and, on the other hand, for our education system to contribute to improving our country's social welfare and growth. Secondly, the 2016 budgets reaffirm the Government's commitment to culture, its industry, its creative potential and its relevance as a common heritage and wealth for our country and for our society. These budgets support major cultural institutions, contribute to the development of cultural and creative industries and establish a stable and predictable incentive system for culture. Culture is a key sector to strengthen our competitiveness and adapt to globalisation. We have been very aware in the design of these accounts that creativity is one of the greatest riches in our country. Contributing to relaunching the economy, Section 18, Education, Culture and Sport to strengthen our image as a country and to improve society as a whole. I would also like to stress today that this creativity has a price, that talent must be rewarded and, therefore, there must be no tolerance for piracy. We must assume the fight against piracy as a social commitment, which I also assume as an educational issue, a question of raising awareness among our young people from school. Ladies and gentlemen, I think we share the idea that culture is a civic necessity, that it is an investment and not an expenditure. With these budgets we have wanted to ensure the sustainability of our country's cultural policy, to ensure its transparency and to move it away from contingency and provisionality. Thirdly, the Ministry's budgets for the year 2016 in the area of sport reinforce the promotion of sports practice at all levels, the protection of the health of the athlete and the fight against doping, control and transparency in the governance of federations and - I would like to stress this because it seems to me very important - support for the equality and integration of women in all areas of sport, especially in the structures of government and management bodies. Ladies and gentlemen, I should like to refer to the first general figures. According to the draft State General Budgets for 2016, the budget allocated to the Ministry of Education, Culture and Sport is 9.64 % higher than in 2015. From 2,862 million euros to 3,138 million in 2016. Of these 3,138 million, 2,139 will go to the education area, 10.80% more than in 2015; 436 million euros will go to the culture area, 12.32% more than in the current year and 161 million euros will go to the sport area, 12.04 % more than in 2015. The share of common services rises by only 0.52 %, after having fallen by 60 % during the parliamentary term thanks to significant savings in common expenses and the ministry's current services. Again this year we want to be deliberately austere in our current spending to allocate our resources to the substantive policies of the ministry. As I was telling you, ladies and gentlemen, the expenditure on education in the ministry's budget for the coming year is 2,139 million euros, 209 million euros more than in 2015. Education will devote the main part of this budget to general scholarships and study grants. In 2016 this item will have the largest budget in history: 1.416,5 million euros. Fellowships represent the Government's commitment to people and to equal opportunities, as lack of economic resources cannot be an obstacle to talent development and knowledge creation. No one in Spain will stop studying for lack of economic resources. For this reason more than 66% of the budget of the Ministry of Education is devoted to social cohesion items, mainly scholarships and grants for the purchase of books and teaching materials, as I will detail below. With the new scholarship model approved during the 2013-2014 academic year, the ministry spends on grants with responsibility, that is, it spends what it has; contrary to what happened in the previous legislature in which a quantity was budgeted and then spent above that item, which caused a debt that was about to collapse the general scholarship system, endangering the call for the 2013-2014 academic year. [[Rumors]] Now the viability of our scholarship system is guaranteed for the present and for the future. Ladies and gentlemen, the quality of an education system rests on its teachers. For this reason, the operative part of the draft General Budgets of the State for the financial year 2016 contains a regulatory proposal with great impact in this area. I am referring to the increase in the replacement rate of staff in the teaching public service to 100 per cent, which is equivalent to the total coverage of new income needs due to retirements and other permanent falls. This proposal puts an end to the constraints that began in 2012 due to the economic circumstances of the moment. This proposal is a fundamental step in our determination and determination to seek the reversal of measures that should have been taken in a crisis scenario that has already been overcome. This measure is particularly important for two reasons. Firstly, because it will lead to a significant improvement in the quality and stability of teaching employment and, secondly, because it implies a 20% drop in the maximum ratios. Article 2 of Royal Decree-Law 14/2012 of 20 April provided that when the State General Budgets Act establishes a replacement rate of less than 50%, educational administrations could extend the maximum number of pupils established for the education regulated by the Organic Law 2/2006 by up to 20%. Since the State General Budgets proposal for 2016 includes a 100 per cent replacement rate, Article 2 of the Royal Decree-Law is not applicable, so the maximum permitted ratio of pupils per classroom is again the one indicated in the basic legislation. Thus, in primary education there is a maximum of 30 to 25 pupils, in compulsory secondary education there is a maximum of 36 to 30 and in baccalaureate, from 42 to 35. In the case of the university, the need to replace permanent falls and retirements is compounded by the fact that the limited replacement rate has been calculating these years also for promotion between universities or within the same university. I am very pleased to tell you that in the royal decree-law adopted in the past Council of Ministers both were suppressed, with which we will have more promotion between universities or within the same university greater mobility. Again, I insist that a short-term measure is thus reversed which the economic recovery scenario allows to redefine. Based on the conviction that no student is left without education due to economic problems, education budgets recover the aid programme for the purchase of missing textbooks and teaching materials in 2012, with the aim of helping families with lower economic resources in the purchase of textbooks and school materials. The ministry will invest 24 million euros in 2016, but this year it will enable an extra 24 million euros to be spent so that families can enjoy these scholarships since the beginning of the 2015-2016 course. At the sectoral conference held on 13 August, we agreed with the autonomous communities that it was the people with the lowest economic resources who could enjoy this new resource. This is an important and decisive first step that shows the government's willingness to recover this aid, which will amount to EUR 51 million in two years, including the EUR 3 million that the ministry will devote to Ceuta and Melilla. On the other hand, the adaptation to the global society in which we live, the international projection of our country and the mobility of our young and future professionals require an ever greater dominance of the main foreign languages. As a result, another of the bets of the Ministry of Education, Culture and Sport is that of foreign language learning, which in the 2016 budget will have an item of 173.5 million euros. The Erasmus programme will have EUR 163.5 million, EUR 126.5 million for the Erasmus+ programme - EUR 2.5 million more than in 2015 -, EUR 37 million for the Erasmus.es reinforcement programme, and another EUR 10 million for immersion courses abroad and in Spain. Another important item is that dedicated to financing the implementation of the Lomce in all the autonomous communities, to which will be dedicated 368 million euros. I would remind you, ladies and gentlemen, that this funding is also being made through the European Social Fund. In conclusion, ladies and gentlemen, these are budgets of deep social content, aimed at improving the quality of our education system and reducing the early school drop-out rate in line with the objectives of the 2020 strategy adopted by the European Union. Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, within the Ministry of Education, Culture and Sport, the cultural budget will reach 436 million euros in 2016, compared to 388 million euros in 2015. We are therefore talking about an increase of 12.32%, which is the largest percentage increase since 2005. Among the actions in this budget year I would like to highlight, first of all, the aid for cinema, which will grow by 65.3% compared to 2015. The priority for the next financial year is the change in the model of aid to the sector. The new system of film aid consists of pre-production aid for feature films on the project, which will coexist with traditional selective project aid. The new aid will allow the progressive financing of film productions during their development, as the sector has been demanding, in the face of amortisation aid, which was called two years after the film's premiere and which has proved to be an unsustainable model in times of budgetary scarcity. This change will reduce the financial costs of films by more than 15 % and will direct the aid to production. The new film financing model will have the necessary funding during the transitional period to deal with the payment of amortisation aid for films released in 2013, 2014 and 2015. Consequently, the budget of the Institute of Cinematography and Audiovisual Arts grows in 2016 by 44.77 %, up to 76.9 million euros. Of this amount, the ICAA will allocate EUR 60,5 million to the aid collected from the Film Protection Fund, which, as I said, is growing by 65,3 %. There will also be an increase in the museum programme by 5.26% to EUR 150.2 million. Within this item the amount to cover the expenses of museums dependent on the Directorate-General for Fine Arts grows by 7.3%, to almost EUR 52 million in 2016. Thus, the budget of the Prado National Museum grows by 7.18% compared to 2015, reaching 45 million euros. The capital of the Reina Sofia Museum also grows by 2.58%, i.e. 36 million euros; the National Library increases its budget by more than 2%, to reach almost 30 million euros; and transfers to the Thyssen Museum are also increased by 12.8%, to the National Museum of Art of Catalonia by 5.08 % and to the Museum of Contemporary Art of Barcelona by 5.08 %. In the field of fine arts, aid for the promotion of contemporary Spanish art amounting to EUR 150,000 was recovered in 2016, which had been abolished as a result of budgetary adjustments in previous years. The game dedicated to performing arts also grows by 15.4%. The budget of the National Institute of Performing Arts and Music, the Inaem, will amount to almost 150 million euros in 2016. The Inaem increases by 3.5% the aid granted to the Teatro Real, the Gran Teatre del Liceu, the Teatro de la Maestranza, Mercat de les Flors and Palau de les Arts of the Valencian Community. In 2016, the ministry has maintained its support for the Platea program, with €5 million, with which it promotes the recruitment of performing arts companies by local entities throughout Spain, which is having a great success. It has also strengthened its contribution to the Ibero-Scene programme by 15% with the aim of contributing to the formation of a common Ibero-American space for performing arts. In short, Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, a considerable budgetary effort is being made in the field of culture. Ladies and gentlemen, the preliminary draft of these General State Budgets for 2016 increases investment for Spanish sport by 12.04 %; in this way, the budget of the Higher Sports Council for the coming year will amount to more than EUR 161 million compared to the EUR 144 million budgeted for 2015. The budget of the Higher Sports Council, excluding the Game Law, amounts to a total of 97.9 million euros, 13.36% higher than the year 2015, which is divided into two headings: 89.3 million euros will correspond to the Higher Sports Council and 8.6 million euros to the Spanish Agency for the Protection of Health and Sport, which represents an increase of more than 38%. I would like to highlight this latest budget increase, because it is a decisive commitment to protecting the health of our athletes and to combating doping in our country. On the other hand, the revenue-based item of the gambling activity tax amounts to EUR 75 million, an increase of 7.14% compared to the forecast for 2015. In this way, the final amount allocated to the Higher Sports Council, including the corresponding portion of the quinielas, is a total of EUR 164.3 million, which is 9.43 % more than in 2015. This budget of the Higher Sports Council has been increased to increase certain budget items of particular interest in the year of the Olympic and Paralympic Games in Rio de Janeiro. Thus, the subsidy to the Spanish sports federations will grow by 23.35 %, the aid to athletes for the results obtained increases by 2.50 %, the subsidy to the Spanish Olympic Committee rises by 316,67 % and the subsidy to the Spanish Paralympic Committee by 400 %. Finally, there is a new budget for university sports, amounting to EUR 200,000, with the aim of maintaining the promotion of sport as an essential part of university life. Similarly, the provision for audits with an amount of EUR 300,000 is strengthened in order to improve the financial controls of the sports federations. And finally, the credits for insularity grow 600,000 euros for clubs, autonomous federations and sportsmen living in the Canary Islands, Balearic Islands, Ceuta and Melilla, with the aim of enabling them to participate on equal terms in non-professional national competitions held in the Peninsula. Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, these budgets I am presenting today want to respond to the demands of students, parents and teachers, to reverse the adjustments made in accordance with the consolidation of public accounts and the commitment of the Government and to strengthen the social action of the Government through educational policy. In the field of culture, our objective with these budgets is to guarantee citizens' access to culture together with the encouragement and protection of cultural industries and the promotion of all artistic manifestations. Finally, these budgets persist in the growing support for all forms of sports activities and in the effort, to which I referred earlier, in the fight against doping. Ladies and gentlemen, we have a broad common field of work on which to build agreements. These budgets look from a present flattering and prepare for everyone a hopeful future. Thank you so much for your attention. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2015-09-16-CD150916.u6	Thank you very much, Mr Azpiazu. For the Group of the Progressive Union and Democracy, Mr Martínez Gorriaran has the floor.
ParlaMint-ES_2015-09-16-CD150916.u7	Thank you, Mr. President. Minister, the explanation or account you have given us of this section of the General Budgets of the State, as I also had the opportunity to say yesterday in other sections, cannot be more continuous with the policy that has existed so far in education. It is true that certain items are recovered, which increase those destined for the replacement of teaching places, which increase that of scholarships, etc., but with conditions and a policy of substance that we do not share at all. I have to say - I have done this throughout this legislature many times - that the educational policy of this government has been one of the biggest disappointments of this legislature for our group and one of the biggest fiascos, because the Lomce - that law that you have also talked about, as it cannot be otherwise, because it should come into force next year, although this is highly unlikely - it is a law that has finally been deepening in most of the problems that the model inherited from the Logse had without changing those fundamental issues that have to do with having an education of the 21st century, an education really concerned with equal opportunities, the development of knowledge and the overcoming of certain pedagogical concepts that have been revealed for too many years as a ballast and a problem for the take-off of education in Spain. The problem is that these budgets, such as Lomce herself, which, as I said, is a law that is going to come into force, we will see how and when, if it actually comes into force - not only because it has very little consensus and depends on the absolute majority of the People's Party, but also because it is a very badly-formed law, filled with all kinds of technical problems, as you yourself know, by the meetings that you have had with the educational community in order to be able to enter into force, regardless of the color of the autonomous governments that take charge of their development - as well as in general the educational conception, do not solve the problems of the lack of a system of education in Spain, that is, of the non-system, which is divided into seven Taifas, into seven educational subsystems, whose differences certainly do not stop growing, and I will immediately make some rather eloquent references to it. Thanks to Lomce, the curricular differences between the different autonomous communities in what is considered basic knowledge - I mean subjects such as mathematics, language, etc. - will increase to 33% in ordinary communities and 50% in communities with two official languages. What are we talking about? We are talking about that students of the same age will have a preparation when they reach a certain level of educational development of up to 33%, which is barbaric. This is not even the case when countries that are members of the OECD are compared, as is also the case in other Spanish educational indicators. The budgets they do are to deepen all this, that is, to divide the little that the State devotes to education into different concepts, leaving in the hands of the autonomous communities their development, autonomous communities that in many cases cannot cope with the costs of the educational system itself. In short, these budgets and in general their educational policy do what they do is to reinforce, deepen and bring even more to the extreme the worst features of the Spanish educational system. For example, you have not made any reference - or at least I do not remember having heard it, that is, if you have done so, to vocational training, when one of the things we say to you that Lomce at least maintained or in what has made a breakthrough is to recognize that vocational training is one of the great failures and defects of the Spanish educational system, which has been oriented towards social promotion rather than towards equal opportunities and attention to the different needs of the educational system and of schoolchildren. It seems that vocational training is of no greater importance. Many other things that you have said also remain in the tinker. Snacks and help? Yes, they do, but because the conditions for obtaining them are not changing, what is happening these years is going to happen, that many of these scholarships and grants are not being implemented, without anyone enjoying them, because what they have done has been to raise the level of the requirements, not as meritocratic requirements, as they have said, no, no, they are barriers of access, not meritocratic requirements. The few references he has made to the university, which is one of the problems that should be dealt with in depth, has also been absolutely disappointing. In fact, in the face of the chaos that this government has brought into the university system, which is going to be of truly biblical proportions because there are universities that have a system of four plus one, another of three plus two, etc., there has been a very eloquent silence that has to do with this breakdown of the university system, which ceases to be a system, as I said, and that is that there has been no reference to the financing of master's degrees. If the master's degrees are not financed by public funds in Spain, the equality of opportunities in higher education simply disappears, because there are going to be many young people from families with few economic means who, as a result of the increase in social inequality, will not have the resources to be able to cope with the costs of master's degrees, which means that they will obtain university degrees of degree that in the labour market will not serve for anything in most cases, especially in those professions that have enabling masters, which you know what they are, means that if you do not have the master's degree you cannot exercise: medicine, certain engineering, advocacy, etc. One of the consequences of the economic crisis, which is already being addressed by major NGOs, is the emergence of a new phenomenon that has come to be called educational poverty, which is nothing more than the consequence of the practice of the disappearance of equal opportunities, because, even if we have universal compulsory education, what good is it for children to go to a classroom if it turns out that they have not been able to have breakfast properly and are not well fed in order to cope with the expenditure of education? You know that the brain - for those who use it and for those who do not use it too, is a curious paradox - is the one that spends most of the energy produced in metabolism. That is, a malnourished child cannot be a good student, it is simply impossible, and there are 33% of children living in Spain, even if you deny it, in a situation of poverty and in danger of social exclusion, and in that you agree from Caritas and Unicef to Save the Children and all kinds of organizations dedicated to these issues. As I said earlier, why do we want classrooms to put children inside if they are malnourished, if their parents can't buy them both textbooks and school materials, etc.? On this I must also say that in this Congress it has been approved twice - one in a non-legislative proposal that we had the honor to present from my group and another in Lomce itself, but without any budgetary consequences - to create a system of lending school books, which is much better than subsidizing the purchase. Subsidizing the purchase only serves to subsidize the powerful editorials of textbooks, which have a captive market and which in turn are in a privileged relationship or in the main communication groups in Spain, which in turn have a privileged relationship with you. In serious and civilized countries, the system that really works well is that of school book loans. This has been approved here and does not appear anywhere in the budgets. Unfortunately there is very little time to be able to talk about an issue as important as ignored in this country - that's how it goes - but I don't want to end without mentioning the truly incredible differences in school indicators between the autonomous communities, which are going to get worse. For example, if we talk about the rate of ninis, that is, young people between eighteen and twenty-four years old who have not finished high school and are neither trained nor worked, it goes from 10% in the Basque Country, which is the minimum, to 30% in the Balearic Islands, to 25% in Catalonia, 20% in Madrid or 12% in Cantabria and others. ParlaMint-ES_2015-09-16-CD150916.u20	Thank you very much, Mrs Moneo. We shall now proceed to the debate on section 16. The Minister of the Interior has the floor on behalf of the Government for his presentation.
ParlaMint-ES_2015-09-16-CD150916.u21	Thank you very much, Madam President. Madam President, ladies and gentlemen, I come once again to this House to present the draft budgets of the Ministry of the Interior, which is integrated into the overall draft general state budgets for the next financial year 2016. As a first general consideration, I would like to point out that, in my view, the budget balance of this Parliament can certainly be regarded as positive. The economic situation that this Government inherited forced, as we all know, adjustment measures that affected, as it could not otherwise be, the Ministry of the Interior, but thanks to these measures, as I had occasion to tell your ladies and gentlemen on the occasion of the presentation and defence of the 2014 budgets, a turning point was already taking place at that time that heralded the path of economic growth and employment generation, a situation that we are already in today. The purpose of the 2016 draft budgets is to work together, as it cannot otherwise be, to consolidate this growth in a context of clear economic recovery, as the forecasts of various international bodies, including national ones, and as the positive evolution of GDP in recent quarters can be seen. This clear improvement in economic indicators is what allows the Ministry of the Interior budget for the next year, including the autonomous bodies and in consolidated terms, to rise to just over €8.2 billion, an increase of 4% over 2015. If we consider only the activities of the Ministry of the Interior without autonomous bodies, the budget is slightly more than 7.4 billion euros, which in this case would mean an increase, compared to 2015, of 3.91%; 4% in one case and 3.91% in the other, of increase in homogeneous terms. As I say to you, in order to reach these figures it is necessary, in fact, to compare both budgets in homogeneous terms; that is, by separately determining the electoral appropriations of each year; slightly more than 345 million euros in the budgets of this year 2015, since we have had - as your lords know - or we will have, at the end of four electoral calls - the Andalusian autonomic elections, the Catalan autonomic elections, the municipal elections and the general elections, while for the next financial year 2016, there are only two calls, in principle, which are those of the Basque Country and Galicia, for whose purpose some 123 million euros are budgeted. In order for the comparison to be in homogeneous terms, first of all, it is clear that these appropriations must be released from the respective budgets for both years, 2015 in one case and 2016 in the other. Secondly, in order to remain a homogeneous comparison, we must add the allocations for centralised recruitment services to the initiatives planned by the CORA, which, for the Ministry of the Interior in 2016, amount to more than EUR 14.3 million. I'll come back briefly on that later. And, thirdly - and I continue with the things we have to do to make the comparison homogeneous and therefore valid - we have to calculate the wage increase of public employees negotiated with trade unions, which is set out in section 31 of the general budgets, and the criterion of stabilising staffing in the public employment offer for 2016, to which I will also refer briefly below. Finally, ladies and gentlemen, the budgetary growth of 4% will undoubtedly contribute to the incipient economic growth that already exists, without neglecting the tools or instruments that the Government has been implementing since the beginning of the legislature in order to get, among all, to reactivate our economy and get out of the recession, such as fiscal consolidation, deficit control and structural reforms to improve productivity and administrative efficiency. I would like to dwell in particular on this last point, where the Ministry of the Interior has made an effort which we can without exaggeration describe as important, ladies and gentlemen, since greater efficiency and productivity should be achieved without diminishing the quality of the services we provide, services which, as we all know, citizens consider essential for their safety and well-being by demonstrating in this way that efficiency and efficiency are not concepts that are contradictory but quite the opposite; they are compatible and complementary. The Government has been sensitive to this fact and proof of this is that the weight of the Ministry of the Interior in all public expenditure of the State has not been reduced during this legislature. In particular, Section 16, Interior for the next financial year 2016 is increased by 3 tenths, from 4.4% in 2015 to 4.7% by 2016. Another economic stimulus for growth is the budgetary investment effort, for which we have forecast an increase of 14.61 %, compared to 2015, with the autonomous bodies and which amounts to 23 % if we exclude those bodies. In productivity improvement initiatives, I would like to highlight the centralised recruitment programme, which involves the transfer of a set of appropriations from Chapter 2, so far managed by the Ministry - cleaning, postal services, telecommunications, etc. - and which go to section 31 of the general budgets to be managed centrally and, consequently, with significant cost savings. These measures are the result of the Commission's work on public administration reform, CORA. As for spending chapters, before I expose them, I want to stop, if I may, in the public employment offer for 2016. The Government's forecast for the corps of State Security Forces and Prison Institutions is to stabilize staffing, which involves the replacement of all casualties, i.e. a 100 per cent replacement rate. With regard to the various expenditure chapters of the budget of the Ministry of the Interior, including the autonomous agencies, the most important figures and variations of each of them are those that I briefly set out to them. With regard to Chapter 1, staff costs, which in a ministry such as that of the Interior, with almost 200,000 people dependent on it, is logically the most important quantitatively of the budget; we are talking about something more than EUR 6,159 million, which represents 76.8% of the total budget of the ministry, but, I repeat, we are talking about Chapter 1, staff costs, in a ministry with almost 200,000 people dependent on it and providing its services in it. This is therefore a chapter that is particularly affected for 2016 because of what I mentioned earlier: the increase in the replenishment rate from 50 % to 100 % for the bodies I have indicated, and the negotiations concluded by the Government with the public employees' unions for the 1% increase in salaries, as well as the commitment to recover 50 % of the extraordinary pay, both of which are reflected in section 31 of these general budgets. [[Civil Guard, National Police and Prison Institutions]] In Chapter 2, current expenditure on goods and services, account must be taken of both the electoral appropriations, to which I referred earlier, and the centralised recruitment initiatives of CORA, both of which must be discounted if the comparison of the budgets for this financial year 2015 and of which I am presenting them for 2016 can be made, as I have repeatedly pointed out, in homogeneous terms in order to be valid. Without electoral appropriations, therefore, nor CORA's centralised recruitment initiatives - therefore, in homogeneous terms - the allocation of Chapter 2 for 2016 is almost EUR 1,403 million, which means an increase of 3,84 % over the current expenditure for 2015, which was EUR 1,351 million. With regard to debt payments, the Ministry has made an important management effort by having managed to avoid and eliminate inadequacies and debts, so that no provision for debt payments is already included in this budget. I would like to remind you, ladies and gentlemen, that at the end of the 2011 financial year - the first one we are with - the Ministry of the Interior presented a living debt amounting to more than 217 million euros, precisely at a time of economic recession, of falling tax revenues, etc. Therefore, we all had to make a special effort to liquidate it; as I say to you, there is no debt in the Ministry of the Interior at this time. In short, ladies and gentlemen, we have managed to clean up the ministry's finances by eliminating that debt which, if it had followed the trend of previous years, would have seriously affected the efficiency and effectiveness of the services to be provided from the department. In short, less debt - now, now, zero - and better service. In chapter 4, current transfers, I have already pointed out to you that we cannot avoid, as is evident in a very significant way, variations in electoral appropriations. Although this chapter has a small weight in the entire budget of the ministry, it is endowed for 2016 with slightly more than 120 million euros; 4.96 million more than in 2015 or, which is the same, an increase of 4,30 %. Chapter 6, real investments, is the one experiencing the greatest growth. It is endowed with almost 289 million euros, an increase of more than 14.6% compared to 2015. These investments will be aimed at improving the quality of life of citizens, reinforcing the services directly related to their security - citizen, road and in the prison -, distributing fundamentally - we are talking about real investments - between the Secretariat of State of Security, 37.5 million euros; National Police, 27 million; Guardia Civil, 38.7 million; Prisons, 10 million; and Directorate General of Traffic, 133.6 million euros. In this area, I would like to highlight the provisions for police stations and civilian guard barracks. Very briefly, Madam President, ladies and gentlemen, I shall now refer to the most relevant spending programmes of the Ministry and its management centres. As regards the programmes entrusted to the Undersecretariat of the Interior, the Undersecretariat will manage a total of 966 million euros, in consolidated terms. The specific civil protection programme, which they know is 134.M - they know for sure, at least the spokespersons and the deputies of the Committee on the Interior - is managed by the Directorate-General for Civil Protection and Emergencies and maintains a similar level of expenditure to 2015. In addition to this programme, as honourable Members know, the appropriations to cover - this is very important - compensation for disasters and emergencies are extendable. On the other hand, I would like to make a special mention of the effort made with all parliamentary groups and to coordinate with the local and autonomous administrations to reach agreement on the new Law 17/2015 of 9 July of the national civil protection system, adopted a few months ago by this House and which will undoubtedly contribute to strengthening our national civil protection system. Following the programmes managed by the Undersecretary of the Interior, we meet with the Directorate-General for Internal Policy, which manages two important programmes. The issue of elections and political parties is affected by the forecast, as I have already mentioned, of conducting two electoral processes in principle next year, in the Basque Country and Galicia - I am not extending there in terms of forecasts, for obvious reasons - and to this end the programme is endowed with something more than 123 million euros. I have already told you that it is 222 million less than by 2015, because this year, as we know, we have had more elections, we will have Catalan autonomous elections on 27 September and before the end of the year general elections. [[municipal and regional authorities]] Therefore, that is what justifies this reduction. The second programme managed by the Directorate-General for Internal Policy, which is very important and is absolutely and rabidly topical - if I may say so - is that of the right of asylum and stateless persons. They are not hidden from their relevance because we are all aware of the humanitarian tragedy, the human exodus that is taking place in various parts of Europe; first it was in the Central Mediterranean, now it is moving towards the Eastern Mediterranean and to Central and Eastern Europe. I take this opportunity to tell you that, if at first - we all have in mind the tragedy of Lampedusa in 2013 - the route of the Central Mediterranean was the one chosen especially by the people who in that human exodus tried to escape from the tragedy, the war and the other conflicts that are taking place in the area, today, as we were told yesterday in the JHA Council of Ministers we had in Brussels, this trend has changed. [[the terrorism of Daesh, etc.]] The proportion until recently was one to three in terms of refugees going through the Central Mediterranean as regards the route of the Aegean Sea, the Eastern Mediterranean, and at the moment it is shifting to a ratio of one to five; that is, of each refugee going from Libya to Italy through the Central Mediterranean four going along the route of the Eastern Mediterranean. I am referring to refugees leaving Turkey, arriving in the Greek islands and from there moving towards the continent, passing through Macedonia, Hungary and following, dramatically, the route we now know through the various media. We are talking about a programme of special relevance right now. In this regard, I would like to stress that in this situation the Government, as it cannot otherwise be, is taking a positive and constructive, supportive and responsible position before Europe, being part of the solution and not part of the problem. This is also acknowledged at the JHA Council of Ministers of the Interior, as I had the opportunity to check it once again yesterday at the meeting we had. We are dealing here with this drama, which, as you know, ladies and gentlemen, has not been agreed because some countries have blocked the decision to be adopted unanimously on a proposal from the Presidency, in this Luxembourg case. We have adopted a position, as it cannot otherwise be in the face of this situation, especially solidarity and constructive, and which, as I say, has been acknowledged to us. It has been recognized that Spain is a referent, and we should all know that because it is so. Spain has become a focal point in the European Union - more and more mentioned in public speeches - on the control of irregular immigration. The fact that neither the Atlantic front nor the Western Mediterranean is currently producing a humanitarian tragedy, as is the case in the Central Mediterranean, the Eastern Mediterranean and Central Europe, is precisely because Spain is acting with solidarity and responsibility. The first to speak yesterday at the JHA Council in Brussels was the President of Acnur, Don António Guterres, who began by referring to Spain by talking about this issue, thanking and publicly recognizing Spain that, with its responsible and solidarity work on the Atlantic front and in the Western Mediterranean and with its collaboration with the countries of origin and transit, specifically Mauritania, Senegal and Morocco, it was preventing these humanitarian tragedies from occurring in that area. What we often do not recognize is being recognized more and more on the outside. I am talking about the President of Acnur, but I can also do this from representatives and ministers of the interior of several very important countries and states of the Union, who, in their public interventions in the JHA Councils over and over again, are reiterating Spain's policy for the control of irregular immigration as a reference model. In fact, the European programme of return to the list of safe countries is an initiative that Spain has repeatedly put on the table at the meetings of the JHA Councils and also at those specialized groups, such as the G-6 or the G-10, which, as you know, deal continuously with these issues which affect both stability and the security of an area as important as the European Union, which must be characterized as an area of freedom, justice, security and solidarity. To this end, ladies and gentlemen, we have made a special effort to ensure compliance with the provisions of Law 12/2009 of 30 October, which regulates the right of asylum and subsidiary protection, establishing a considerable increase of more than 105 % over 2015. On the other hand, as you know very well because you have actively and positively contributed to this and in order to respond to the forecasts for the reception of refugees which we estimate in figures which I will now give you, in agreement with all the parliamentary groups - the Minister for Employment and Social Security has already explained - in the context of the heading relating to the international protection system, which initially in the draft budget was endowed with EUR 24,125,000, an additional EUR 200 million has been added by way of amendment. Thus, at present we have for the international protection system an allocation of 229 million euros - in short, it is a question of providing the necessary resources in the budgets for the reception and integration of these refugees - which in any case can be extended with extraordinary credits if it is considered necessary given the so dynamic evolution of the events. As I am speaking on this issue, as your ladies and gentlemen know, on 9 and 20 July there were two JHA Councils, which discussed the European Commission's proposal for a decision on two programmes; one of them was the resettlement of 20,000 people. You know that resettlement means placing people who are susceptible to asylum or international protection outside the territory of the European Union; they are people who have not arrived in the European Union, who are in refugee camps, mainly in Jordan and Lebanon. This is one of the resettlement programmes in which Spain has been collaborating in a special way since 2010 - the possibility of extending it to other countries such as Turkey, etc. - to resettle 20,000 of these refugees, and quotas were set for each of the twenty-eight States of the Union; there were also talks about 40,000 resettled, people who could claim asylum or international protection and who had already arrived in the European Union, specifically Greece and Italy. The criterion is that the nationals to whom such a relocation programme would apply would initially be Syrians and Eritreans exclusively, with the possibility of extending it to third-country nationals who had obtained at least 75 per cent of the grant in asylum applications when such nationals were concerned. For the time being, we are talking about Italy and Greece as regards countries where hotspots should be installed in which those people should be identified and those who are economic immigrants should be differentiated from those who are refugees because of war or any other issue that might eventually lead to such a request for asylum and the corresponding grant. Speaking of Greece and Italy, and of the installation of these hotspots, the JHA Council of the day before yesterday was concrete in the proposal of conclusion - not achieving unanimity, it has not yet been approved; in order to try to achieve it we will meet again next Tuesday, this time in Luxembourg - that today, 16 September, the five hotspots that it has planned Italy had to come into operation - one in Lampedusa, one in the south of the peninsula, in Puglia, and three in Sicily - plus that of Greece in the port of Piraeus, in Athens. To date they had to be put in place so that this relocation programme could start to be implemented on a transitional and voluntary basis - I am talking about last July - and for a period of two years. As you know, the 40,000 relocation programme allocated quotas that took into account population variables, gross domestic product, efforts to contain irregular immigration and the unemployment rate of each country. That meant an allocation to each of the countries, which, being voluntary in nature, led to that figure being obtained for the 20,000 people who could be resettled, and too much, yet we stayed at 32,237 compared to the 40,000 to relocate. It is clear that this is no problem because it is a two-year programme, if today we already have more than 32,000, it is clear that there is no problem so that in the course of this temporary and voluntary two-year programme the 7,700 missing will be achieved. In fact, the Commission's agreement was that by the end of the year we will reach a pact to see how we distribute those 7,700 potentially refugee people and that they will consolidate the 40,000 figure to be relocated, agreed voluntarily by all the States - I shall now refer to that - that we are on that programme. For these programmes we are not the 28 Member States of the European Union. As you know, there are three countries - Ireland, the United Kingdom and Denmark - that are opting in, opting out, and therefore are in their right to benefit from them or not. In principle, the United Kingdom, Ireland and Denmark are not voluntarily integrated into the relocation, but they have finally stated that, although they are in the opting out, they will cooperate; they have made offers of accommodation. Then there are four countries that are part of the Schengen border code and are not part of the European Union, but are involved in that programme. [[Switzerland, Liechtenstein, Iceland and Norway]] We are therefore not strictly talking about the twenty-eight states of the Union, but about the twenty-six Schengen states most voluntarily some states. The summary of everything is that we are in the front row, despite what is said, regarding the voluntariness of the programs of July 2011 of 20,000 resettled and 40,000 relocated. Before yesterday the European Parliament, President Juncker, presented the proposal of 120,000 relocated on a compulsory basis, no longer just for Greece and Italy, but it is left unconcrete to see whether or not Hungary will eventually enter as well. In any case, it is intended to be non-transitional, but there is the possibility that it will be voluntary, which would require amending the Dublin Minutes. That has a great political significance and explains that the discussions, the debates are intense, because, if we want to do it right and we have to change such fundamental issues as the Dublin agreement in order to reach a unanimous pact, by participating in the European Parliament the process is particularly complex. All that said, Spain has accepted without reservation the quota allocated to us in this last program of 120,000 relocated, which is almost 15,000 to relocate as potential refugees fleeing the war or for other reasons that, as provided for in international treaties, could justify the request and the granting of asylum or international protection. In our case they would amount to 15,000, which in addition to those we already counted in July would lead us to 18,000. Apart from these programmes, given the geostrategic situation in Spain, it is important to keep this in mind, on the border of Beni Ensar, in Melilla, and in the Tarajal, in Ceuta, this year, I inaugurated asylum and shelter offices so that people who want to reach Spain, therefore to the European Union, through Melilla or Ceuta, which are also Schengen areas, could do so, in addition to making it clear that those who try to jump the fences illegally are not potential illegal but irregular immigrants for economic reasons that are not entitled to asylum. Those who have it - mainly there come Syrians in the vast majority - have asylum offices there. What we have been doing for the past year is already thousands and thousands of people who have applied for asylum at the Asylum and Refugee Office on the Beni Ensar border in Melilla, one of the four borders that Melilla has with Morocco, as your ladies and gentlemen know. We are growing at a rate of 50 requests per day. If we continue this way in Ceuta, and especially in Melilla, as well as in other units of the Peninsula, mainly in the Asylum and Refugee Office, the CIEs and also in the delegations and sub-delegations of the Government in the units dedicated to asylum applications, in view of the latest developments, the estimate is that at the end of the year 12,000 people have applied for asylum in Spain, which together with the 5,000 requests from Dublin would lead us to between 17,000 and 18,000 more asylum applications, which would triple those of the last year and would have to be added to the programmes of the European Union and the Commission to which I have referred earlier. Today we would be talking about a commitment this year of more than 35,000 asylum seekers, and that without counting the irregular immigrants on whom Spain is working in a very special way, securing our borders in the Atlantic façade and in the area of the Western Mediterranean. I am sorry that I have been extended at this point, but it is so topical and topical, it is a matter of concern and shock to the public with such intensity that, to the extent that it is up to the Ministry of the Interior together with the Ministry of Employment and Social Security to manage this reality, it was justified that I should extend a little in this matter. I turn to another area, which concerns the victims of terrorism. I take this opportunity to say that yesterday I had the opportunity to participate in a particularly emotional and important event. In the prison of Martutene, in San Sebastián, a tribute ceremony was held for the first time in Spain to officials of penal institutions, specifically to officials killed by ETA serving in the prison of Martutene, in San Sebastián. [[Applauds]] It must be extremely hopeful for all - I insist, for all - that all the authorities and a representation of all the political forces were present. They were leaders of the People's Party, of the Socialist Party, but also of course of the PNV and of the Abertzale left, in addition to authorities - from the security adviser of the Basque Government, to the mayor of San Sebastián, to the deputy general of Guipúzcoa, to the president of the General Councils of Guipúzcoa, etc.; all the judicial authorities of the Basque Country and the province of Guipúzcoa, etc. -. In other words, there was a very qualified representation, institutional, political, judicial and of the victims of terrorism and their families. It was unthinkable that such a fact could occur at the moment - I repeat - in a prison such as that of Martutene in San Sebastián, paying tribute, with all the parliamentary bow and all the highly qualified institutional representation, to officials of that prison killed by ETA. That was yesterday morning. It is very hopeful because I was reminded by the Secretary General of Prison Institutions that on the day that he was killed by ETA in 1997 Gómez Elósegui, one of those who yesterday honored themselves as a victim of terrorism, made a simple tribute, a minute of silence in the entrance hall to the prison but there were only three people in that act. Yesterday, as I say, there was no shortage of those who had to pay tribute to those three prison officials who were serving in Martutene prison and who were killed by ETA. I really believe, ladies and gentlemen, that it is important to put it into value, to make it manifest. [[Applauds]] Attention to victims of terrorism is, of course, a responsibility, competence and priority for the Ministry of the Interior; priority that we have given to the implementation of Law 29/2011 on recognition and comprehensive protection of victims of terrorism, adopting the regulations that develop it by Royal Decree 671/2013 of 6 September. I would like to remind you, ladies and gentlemen, that a total of 5,231 applications for aid and compensation have been processed during this parliamentary term and up to this September, and a total of EUR 117.3 million has been paid for this purpose. The Directorate-General for Support to Victims of Terrorism manages two additional credits. The first is that of compensation, aid and subsidies derived from that law, and is expandable in nature, so regardless of the initial appropriation, all obligations arising from the new regulations for the development of the law will undoubtedly be met. This Directorate also manages a specific appropriation for grants to associations, foundations and non-profit entities whose aim is to represent and defend the interests of victims of terrorism, which we maintain for this financial year 2016 at EUR 880,000. I conclude this reference to the victims of terrorism by mentioning the initiative to establish and set up the memorial centre for the victims of terrorism. It is a strategic project for this ministry and derives from a legal mandate, because it is created by article 57 of the Law on the Recognition and Comprehensive Protection of Victims of Terrorism. I would like to recall that this law was adopted unanimously by this House and was the result not of a bill but of a joint bill proposed by the different parliamentary groups. Therefore, the memorial centre for victims of terrorism is a priority of this ministry that derives from a unanimous legal mandate of this House. As I had the opportunity to say yesterday in the act to which I referred, this memorial centre must be constituted as the vault key of the building that must contribute to ensuring the principles that inform this law of memory, truth, dignity and justice for the victims of terrorism and it feels the basis of the account, of the historical truth of what happened in the Basque Country and in the whole of Spain during these years of lead, of decades of ETA terrorism, so that on this historical truth a true peaceful coexistence and freedom worthy of such name is established in the Basque Country and, of course, in all areas where ETA terrorism has created nothing but suffering, death and pain. For the rehabilitation works of this memorial center, which is already underway in what was the headquarters of the Banco de España in Vitoria, we have a endowment for the next year of 1.7 million euros, thus increasing by almost 550.000 euros the endowment regarding this year 2015. As regards road safety - moving to another programme - the Directorate-General for Traffic has a budget of just over €754 million, with an increase of 2.78 %. We especially increase the resources to enhance the traffic monitoring and management systems of the Civil Guard Traffic Group. As for the Secretariat of State for Security, its budget amounts in consolidated terms to just over €7 billion, with an increase of 0.56% compared to 2015, although in terms of comparison - which, as we know, is how it should be - the increase is 3,90 %. We do so from the conviction that investing in security in a democracy, logically, is investing in freedom and guaranteeing the rights of citizens and their well-being. Of the centres under the Secretariat of State for Security, the most important programme is that of citizen security, programme 132, which manages the Secretariat of State for Security, the Directorates-General of the Police and the Civil Guard and the Management of Infrastructure and State Security Equipment. This programme is endowed with € 5.3 billion, with an increase of 0.44% over 2015. As in other Ministry programmes, it highlights the increase in real investments, with an increase of more than 20% compared to 2015, which means more than 20 million euros more in real investments in the field of the Secretariat of State for Security. We have increased investment in infrastructure, particularly investment in the integrated external surveillance system, SIVE, which is so important for combating irregular immigration, for combating drug trafficking and for combating terrorism, as you know. Investment is increased by 30%, distributed by 42% for the fight against irregular immigration, another 42% for the fight against drug trafficking by sea and organised crime, 25% for police equipment and 58% for furniture and supplies. With regard to the operational and operational capacity of the National Police and the Civil Guard, I would like to refer to two issues. One, as I have already said, stabilizes the staff of these State Security Bodies, in addition to nearly 25,000 prison officials. Likewise, the instruments and means of work are increased, the current expenditure chapter of the management centres grows by more than 12.5 million euros in 2016, and in this regard I would like to stress that although it is true that the budgetary resources of the last few years - for reasons we all know - have been smaller, not for that reason - and that is important to highlight - it has diminished the effectiveness and the work carried out by the State Security Forces. There is a data, when we arrived at the government the crime rate in Spain was, according to data from the Eurostat statistical office, 48.4 - i.e. 48.4 criminal offences, crimes and faults, per 1,000 inhabitants - and by June this year we are at 44.3, which places us in the third country of the European Union with the lowest crime rate or, in a positive way, with better security rate. The CIS annual consolidated historical series surveys have also confirmed these objective facts in that, for example, in 2011 the Spanish concern for this issue was 5.6% while in the barometer last June it fell to less than half, to 2.7%. On the other hand, we make a determined bet to strengthen the capabilities of the Counter-Terrorism and Organized Crime Intelligence Centre, merging the existing National Counter-Terrorism Coordination Centre and the Centre for Counter-Organized Crime Intelligence, from the conviction that there is a clear link between terrorism and organized crime, which not a few of its manifestations - such as drug trafficking, or arms trafficking, among others - serve to finance terrorism. We also make a determined bet on resources by the National Center for Critical Infrastructure Protection, known as Cnpic, considering both agencies as basic in safeguarding our freedoms and in a very special way against the new threats arising from international terrorism, from jihadist terrorism. On the other hand, as your ladies and gentlemen know, last February 2 in La Moncloa the President of the Government, Mariano Rajoy, signed with the Secretary-General of the Socialist Party the agreement to strengthen unity in defence of freedoms and in the fight against terrorism, and within that agreement, in addition to the reform of the Penal Code - which entered into force on July 1st - it also included providing our intelligence community and our counter-terrorism services with greater resources to be more effective and better able to deal with that threat. Royal Decree-Law 6/2015 of 14 May granted the Ministry of the Interior, in the context and in accordance with that signed agreement to which I referred, an extraordinary credit of EUR 10 million to enhance the fight against terrorism. I can assure you that they will be very well used and were very necessary. With regard to the heading for training, there is a very substantial increase, amounting to more than EUR 403 million, with a 163 % increase over the previous budget, that of this year 2015. This will allow us to improve and expand the training plans that are made available to members of the State Security Forces and Bodies. We significantly increase the nominative subsidy in favour of the Spanish Red Cross for the realization of programs of existential character in the CIEs, so that tomorrow I will sign an agreement with the Red Cross for its assistance in the CIEs of Barcelona and Madrid, for the moment, but with this increase of credit for the year 2016 we will be able to extend it to all the CIEs that currently operate in the territory. In this way, the Red Cross will be particularly present in the assistance in all areas that correspond to this so respected and respectable institution to care for immigrants who are there in compliance with the provisions of the law and always, as your lords know, with authorization and judicial supervision. As far as international cooperation is concerned, almost EUR 14 million in credits for international cooperation are maintained between direct police cooperation aid and training aid in the field of cooperation. Ladies and gentlemen, this is very important today. When he spoke earlier about cooperation with Morocco, operational cooperation with Senegal and Mauritania, without these programmes we could not do so. In Dakar, in Nuakchot and in Nuadibú we have deployed naval and air units of the Civil Guard and joint investigation teams in the land of Police and Civil Guard that, together with the Senegalese services in Dakar and with the Mauritanians in Nuakchot and in Nuadibú, are securing the borders, avoiding, as I have said, those humanitarian dramas that occur in other areas and at the same time preventing the exit of irregular immigrants and fighting against drug trafficking, which in a very special way, and coming from the Andean area, tries to reach that area mainly with cocaine. All that would not be possible if we did not have these programs and resources. It's okay that when we talk about numbers, we know what's behind it, and that's why I wanted to emphasize it. With regard to the programme of prison centres and institutions, it has a budget of EUR 1,150 million, with a growth of 2.23% over the current financial year. I conclude, ladies and gentlemen, my intervention - forgive me for the extension, but I have had to expand on the issue of the victims of terrorism, and especially on the issue of asylum and protection of refugees, for obvious reasons, because the issue deserved it - saying that the budget of the Ministry of the Interior for 2016 continues with the policy of cleaning up public accounts, but at the same time incorporates the objective of economic recovery. That is why we can assess - we say from the Ministry of the Interior - very positively the increase indicated at the beginning of 4%, in homogeneous terms, compared to the 2015 budget. Ultimately, the public services that from the Ministry of the Interior - almost 200,000 public servants - provide to all citizens are essential and therefore must be adequately equipped, although from the indispensable solidarity and co-responsibility they must be made compatible with the objectives of fiscal consolidation and economic recovery to which I have referred. I am convinced that whoever manages these budgets next year will be in a position for the Ministry of the Interior to continue to provide the Spanish citizens with a great service, guaranteeing their freedom and their rights, which is the mandate that the Constitution, in article 104, entrusts in a very special way to the bodies that depend on this department. Thank you very much, Madam President, ladies and gentlemen. [[Applauds]]
ParlaMint-ES_2015-09-16-CD150916.u22	Thank you very much, Minister. Mrs Díez has the floor in defence of amendments by the Group of the Progressive Union and Democracy.
ParlaMint-ES_2015-09-16-CD150916.u23	Thank you, Madam President. Minister, I would like to make a brief reference to an aspect that has taken up much of your speech, which has been called the refugee crisis, which is not really such, but rather a democratic crisis. Refugees are the fever, but the disease is the lack of democracy, the lack of freedoms, the violation of human rights, the persecution of minorities in the territories, in the countries from which these refugees come. Therefore, as to a democratic crisis and as to a problem of democracy, it is like all countries - and of course ours - that have to face this situation. He has told us about the problems he had at the meeting a couple of days ago and has stressed that Spain, our Government, is having a constructive and positive attitude. With regard to that attitude which you have described as positive and exemplary for the rest of the countries of the European Union, I would like to ask you, on behalf of my group, a couple of questions, because I do not have time for more. The first of these, which is very important, is whether it plans its ministry to activate the development of the regulations of the Asylum Act of 2009. You know, Minister, that the 2009 law is only partially developed in a very timely way and that the regulation that is in force in Spain in relation to asylum develops a law of the 80 and not the 2009 law. So, do you intend to do that? Will they do so immediately and urgently to respond to this need? Also on this same issue, and apart from what is decided at Community level, my group would like to know whether the Government has already begun the preparations necessary to ensure the integration of refugees and, in addition, family reunification. I do not know whether or not there will be agreement at European level, whether it will be binding or whether we will be awarded fair quotas or zeta, but we are going to host refugees, are we, Minister? Spain will host refugees with the intention of integrating them and seeking family reunification. Since we are going to do so, whether there is agreement at European level or not, have we put the procedures in place? They are not the CIEs that you have cited as one of the examples. The family reunification and the reception of refugees, who come to stay - as everyone knows - and who will have all the rights when they arrive in Spain - I will refer only to Spain - like the rest of the citizens - the same rights, except the right to vote - means that they must be taken care of if they are not to be sent to ghettos or CIEs. This means, for example, educating children, preparing educators and having school materials in their language, i.e. making decisions. Since we're going to welcome them, we're going to have to make decisions. In that regard, I would like to ask you whether the Government is already taking decisions to fulfil its obligation to respond to this democratic emergency. I insist that it is not a humanitarian emergency, but a democratic one. The humanitarian emergency is the consequence, the fever, but the real emergency that the Spanish and all Europeans face is the lack of democracy and respect for human rights in all those countries where refugees come from. I'm going to stick to the rest of the time I have on your ministry budget. Minister, I don't have time to go into all the elaborate details of numbers you've given. I will refer exclusively to your last pronouncement. You have said that it is about ensuring essential public services and that the security and freedom of all Spaniards, which to a large extent also depends on the work of those who are attached to your ministry, are guaranteed. You have said that you must be properly endowed, but the Ministry of the Interior is not adequately endowed. Minister, in the 2012 budget - the first one you did - the allocation was 8,610 million euros and in this budget for 2016 is 8,153; that is, 467 million less. It is not well equipped to meet its demands and responsibilities. Until last year, every time we told them this, they were telling us about the crisis and the economic situation. Today they assume that we have emerged from the crisis and that we are the champions of Europe in growth, but the budget remains ridiculous, inadequate to meet the needs of the service. Secondly, Minister, the adequacy is not only the number, the millions of euros, but also the staffing. You in your speech have - let me say - presumed that places will be called and that the templates will be filled. Let's tell the full truth, minister, because if you don't tell the complete truth, you're lying. There are 3,865 less police officers than in 2011 and they have called - if I don't have the numbers wrong; that I don't have the numbers wrong - 1,300 places. Well, you don't cover the templates, you don't cover the needs. The same goes for the Civil Guard: there are 2,833 fewer troops than when you arrived in the government and 820 seats are called. Do not tell us that you are already covering the templates, that at this time there is a 100 percent replacement, because it is not true, then you are distorting reality to boast of something you are not doing. The staff are not covered, nor are the officers of the Police and the Civil Guard, all those responsible for ensuring our security and our freedom, adequately provided from an economic point of view. Minister, the Civil Guard and the National Police have to speak well, but above all we have to act well, we have to stop messing with them and we have to work to ensure that they have sufficient resources to do their job well, to develop their obligation well and to do their job well, because they continue to work well at the expense of carrying on their backs the lack of coverage of vacancies and the lack of adequate remuneration. Finally, one that is a classic in this rostrum and in this debate: the approval between the Civil Guard, the National Police, the Autonomous Polices and the Municipal Polices. I remember with nostalgia the debates of the past legislature in which the People's Group came out and passed us on the left - let me say the colloquial - to everyone asking for approval and ensuring that, as soon as they arrived, that approval would take place.
