This fact, coupled with the reduction in the financial burden of public debt and the lower need for unemployment expenditure, has led to an increase in social expenditure - excluding unemployment - of 3,8 %. Budgets in which almost 54 out of 100 euros are allocated to social policies; 39 out of 100 to pensions; 7 to the payment of different unemployment benefits; and another 8 out of 100 euros are allocated to other social expenditure. In addition, the budgets allocate an additional EUR 17 of these 100 to contributions to the Autonomous Communities, which are mainly devoted to the financing of health, education and social services provided by these administrations. This is therefore social protection, because there are no more social budgets than those that invest in employment, strengthen basic services and protect families that do not yet notice recovery. [[Applauds]] Ladies and gentlemen, the government's social commitment is reflected in the budget of this ministry, which is growing next year by 5.3% to reach EUR 2,021 million. Of these, 80% make up the Social Services and Equality budget, which has increased by more than 6% over 2015. This ensures the various specific support measures and programmes that we have developed since the beginning of this legislature, with a view to those at risk of exclusion: families, minors, women - especially those who are victims of abuse -, older persons, those who have some kind of disability, those who are in a situation of dependency, and young people, especially those who have not obtained a job. The idea is that no one can and does not have to stay behind at this new stage of growth and employment, and that is the goal pursued by the Government. One of the main priorities of the ministry is therefore families and children. This year we have a budget of more than EUR 193 million, which represents an increase of more than 10% over last year. If we add this increase from this year to the increase of more than 20% in the 2015 budget, we are talking about an increase of around 35% in the 2015-2016 budget in this area. We prioritize family policy, we prioritize policies to support children and we do so by raising allocations in this area, once the economic situation also allows it. This is a line that we must consolidate for the future if we are to consider that those most affected by the economic crisis have surely been the families with children in our country. For this reason, the specific fund for these families with children who are in difficult situations is extended to EUR 48 million. It is 50% more than in the current year. Our goal is to make it more effective and to reach local entities through the autonomous communities. This support is reinforced by other appropriations under other headings, such as grants from the IRPF, which provide for a Section 26, Health, Social Services and Equality EUR 100 million for this objective. In total, at this year's call we will distribute 220 million euros, which means an increase of 650,000 over last year. It also highlights the food aid that has a budget of 662 million euros until 2020 and which also includes social support measures that are incorporated into the budget of our ministry this year. Secondly, I would like to highlight some of the actions of another of the major budgetary priorities, namely the care of the elderly and the care of those in dependency. 62% of the ministry's total budget is intended to consolidate the Unit Act, which means an increase in the weight of this priority by 5.9 points more than in the previous legislature. In particular, we will allocate EUR 1,252 million, 6.4% more than in 2015. Together with the transfers of the Ministry of Employment and Social Security we have reached a budget of 3,909 million euros, an additional 123.5, which means that the budget of the Imserso for this year 2016 also grows by 3,26 %. The budgets therefore consolidate the priority of care for the unit and guarantee without any exclusion the subjective right of persons assessed as dependent to receive a benefit. I would like to remind you that we have completed the timetable for the implementation of the law with the incorporation of the 300,000 moderate dependents last July, whose level of coverage already reaches 14% and which will be progressively incorporated by the autonomous communities, I hope as quickly as possible. As you know, credit has been extended every year to meet the State's obligations in this area, and this year too will be the year of the extension of credit in the coming weeks. From there, at the end of this term, it is appropriate to make a thorough assessment of the evolution of the law, which has meant its implementation and to open a debate that will allow us to strengthen it in the future. In addition, the budget allocated to social tourism and thermalism programmes, which promote active ageing, is growing. These are programmes which, as your ladies and gentlemen know, give rise to nearly 11 million hotel stays, generate 447 million euros and maintain more than 100,000 jobs in the sector. On this occasion, the programmes will grow to a budget of 105 million euros and also increase the number of places, which in the case of social tourism will increase from 938,000 to more than one million and in the case of thermalism will reach 210,000. The network of reference centers of the Imserso will increase significantly, it will almost double by the year 2016 with the incorporation of the Center of State Reference for Persons with Severe Neurological Disabilities Stephen Hawking of Langreo. I want to say that there is a departure to finish the works, for operating expenses, to ensure their start-up - it is an important commitment - and for the final launch this year of the reference centers of Valencia, for the psychosocial care of people with serious mental disorder; of Soria, for the socio-health care of people in a situation of dependence; and of León, for the care of people in a situation of dependence. [[Applauds]] In total, the network of Imserso dependency centres will move from the current five centres to nine reference centres in different autonomous communities over the course of this year and next. In addition, the resource centre for the promotion of personal autonomy in Melilla is scheduled to be opened, which also has its corresponding budget allocation. I want to say that the appropriations allocated to both Melilla and Ceuta by the Imserso also have an increase of 3.37% this year. Another priority of the next year's accounts is to continue investing to achieve or bring us closer to real equality between men and women, with particular emphasis on combating gender-based violence. The budget is growing by 6.32% this year, to EUR 25.23 million in the case of the Government Delegation for Gender Violence, and over the last two years we will have increased by more than 13%. Priority: comprehensive assistance to women victims and their children. The game, the money that grows the most is for social assistance programs of the autonomous communities and for the implementation of personalized plans. For the same purpose, the allocation for social assistance, which women receive under article 27 of the Integral Act, is increased by more than 70 per cent. Similarly, we are consolidating prevention and social awareness measures, which produce effective results. They know that the fundamental increase in this game for awareness campaigns took place in the same year 2015 - they have risen by 225 % - and next year it is planned to maintain that effort, which is important and has an impact on the awareness of Spanish society. In addition, progress is planned in other actions such as the promotion of women's employment and the reduction of the wage gap, or in measures to make progress on reconciliation and co-responsibility. These are the main axes of the work of the Institute for Women and Equal Opportunities, which next year will have a budget of EUR 19.7 million. In the case of persons with disabilities, which is the other priority chapter of the ministry, first of all, I would like to highlight the efforts being made to maintain the activity of the social entities of the third sector, at the state level, which have been threatened this year, amounting to EUR 4.1 million through the nominal subsidies provided for in the State General Budgets. We are committed to maintaining and strengthening the third sector, to keeping them out of the way and to strengthening what is a basic protection network for all Spanish citizens, which guarantees cohesion in our country. In the area of youth, we have a budget of EUR 29 million, which we basically aim to promote youth employment. There is an important programme, which this year also has a budget increase which is to be highlighted: Erasmus Youth. With regard to the fight against drug dependence and other addictions, we have a credit in the Government Delegation for the National Drug Plan, which increases slightly to around EUR 15 million, allowing us to consolidate the programmes deriving from the provisions of the regulation of the Fund for property confiscated by illicit drug trafficking. They are expandable appropriations that are doubling the initial budget each year. This year has doubled and the final amount we have set aside has been EUR 28.6 million. Ladies and gentlemen, in the area of health and consumption, the Ministry's aim is to ensure that the National Health System is cohesive and remains an example of equity, quality and sustainability. In 2015, the Ministry of Health has a budget of €370 million for our major priorities: digital health, improved care, efficiency and support for professionals, as well as consumer protection and food security. On this basis we advance in the greatest challenge of the future, which is personalized medicine and social health care for which we have been demanding a great political and social pact for a long time, and working to lay the foundations that make it possible; a pact that we have already signed with healthcare professionals. I want to say that professionals are the pillar of the National Health System and, therefore, in 2016 there are some priorities such as improving the specialized training system, but also speeding up recognition and accreditation procedures. We are culminating - it is very close to its completion - the regulation of the use and indication of medicines by the nursing profession and the entry into operation of the state registry of health professionals. Other challenges that have to culminate over the coming months are the development of truncality and the equivalence of categories. The budget we allocate to public health amounts to 38 million euros. We focus on actions for the prevention of HIV infection and the implementation of the various strategies being implemented by the ministry. Next year we foresee a major advance in the strategy for addressing chronicity with projects such as population stratification and chronic disease management. It will also involve an evaluation and updating of other strategies such as mental health, chronic obstructive pulmonary disease, stroke, diabetes, musculoskeletal diseases, palliative care and ischemic heart disease, as well as the implementation of the new patient safety strategy, which has already been approved by the Interterritorial Council of the National Health System. We also continue with the effort in the provision for digital health, mainly for the extension of the electronic prescription and digital clinical history and, above all, for its interconnection. Interconnection has already begun, but it has to be extended significantly throughout the next exercise. I would like to remind you that the budgets have a specific item - I always think it is important to say this - for the strategy of rare diseases, rare diseases and support for their families. Next year, the Rare Disease Registry has to be fully operational. [[Applauds]] As a novelty this year, there is a party that has been incorporated and is called the resettlement and relocation of refugees from the point of view of health. There has been a significant increase in credit in the Ministry of Employment, but there is also an open allocation in the Ministry of Health, where we also want to help meet the demand and humanitarian crisis that has affected the whole of the European Union and in which Spain also wants to assume its responsibility. In the portfolio area I want to say that the Strategic Plan for Hepatitis C, which is going to be at a good pace, will also require an update in the coming months. Innovations are taking place dynamically with the emergence of new medicines and this will allow us to accelerate and continue to prioritize access to more patients. They know that we currently have a provision for 52,000 people. More than half of these 52,000 people have already received their treatment and therefore an effective response is being given, but this plan can still be further updated with the incorporation of new drugs. In order to make it possible for innovations to arrive, solutions and alternatives are given to diseases that previously did not have them and, therefore, we can improve the effective protection we do of health, we must continue to act on the price system and centralised purchases, which in this legislature have resulted in savings of EUR 5 billion. That is, being effective, being efficient, being sustainable is what allows us to then face challenges such as hepatitis C. For its part, the Spanish Agency for Medicinal Products and Sanitary Products has a budget of 47 million euros. I must say that this agency has this budget but generates €70 million for the Treasury, which derive from the competitive management it carries out with other European agencies. It is an agency that enters more than it spends and that will also continue to make progress in ensuring accessibility to medicines, the correct supply and the fight against counterfeiting; the agency is a model at European level of good management and safety for all specific citizens and will continue with its plans. There is a very important plan, the National Antibiotic Resistance Plan, involving autonomous communities and scientific societies. This year we will continue to develop these specific strategies that will prevent us all. The Spanish Agency for Consumption, Food Security and Nutrition has a budget of 29 million euros. An important part, over 13 million, is devoted to protecting consumers and users, promoting associationism, specialized training and supporting the arbitration system; and the other part is devoted to food and nutrition security, mainly for the development of the NAOS strategy, for the prevention of obesity, and for the investment effort in food safety laboratories. Ingesa's budget also increases by more than 3%. In the case of health infrastructure in Ceuta and Melilla, investments will grow above 9 %. We also plan to have this year's budget in Ceuta and Melilla to increase the recruitment of staff in both specialized and primary care and, to increase the resolution capacity for the movement of patients to the peninsula. I would also like to quote the National Transplant Organization, which is also going to see its budget increase this year. It continues to record world records, and this year we have already met our goal of bone marrow donation, as we were looking for 170,000 donors. With the effort that has been made in this legislature we are improving budgets year after year; last year and this year in a very important way in the area of health, social services and equality policies. Budgets are important because they will consolidate this new stage of economic growth, job creation and strengthening of welfare services and the Government's ability to commit to the situation of families. More social spending to further reduce the levels of inequality and the risk of exclusion that exists in our country. In addition, increased attention to dependence and chronicity in order to cope with the increase in life expectancy, i.e. with quality and guarantees in the context of active ageing. There is more budget to further improve the National Health System and to make it possible to consolidate and strengthen policies for real equality between men and women and the eradication of gender-based violence. These are budgets that are built to ensure the credibility, strength of our country, stability and quality of public services, as well as the well-being of all Spaniards. Thank you very much, Mr President. Thank you very much, ladies and gentlemen. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2015-09-16-CD150916.u77	Thank you, Madam President. The House is not the building where the royal family lives, it is an institution that collaborates with his majesty to fulfill the important responsibilities assigned to him by the Constitution, and the royal family is institutionally composed only of six people: his majesty the kings, his royal Highness the princess of Asturias; his royal Highness the Infanta Sofia and his majesty the Emeritus kings Don Juan Carlos and Dona Sofia, for whom, as a sign of gratitude for what they have done for Spain for four decades, I dare to ask for an ovation. [[Applauds]] The 1995 amendment of the Plural Left proposes that the budget of the House of His Majesty the King be reduced by 20%, without giving any more reason than that it will have a budget more than enough. It does not know, or does not want to acknowledge, that from 2010 the budget has been reduced year after year until 2013, with a reduction of 13.20 % and that the budget has been frozen since then, repeating the same amount in 2014, 2015 and the current 2016. This amendment to the Plural Left is of supine injustice. With the 1965 amendment, the Plural Left pursues two objectives. One, that the members of the Royal House be assimilated to the high offices of the General Administration of the State in respect of the obligations envisaged in relation to the records of activities of property and property rights. The other, that the budgets are presented with disaggregation for precise budget terms to know the distribution. The first thing is to remind The Plural Left once again of Article 65 of the Constitution, which allows the king freely to distribute the budget. And despite this constitutional facility, there is an agreement between the General Intervention of the State and the House of His Majesty the King, dated 18 September 2014, in which, among other commitments, in addition to the audit report of the accounts, it is stipulated that the General Budgets of the State will apply, with the necessary adjustments resulting from the constitutional configuration of the House of His Majesty the King, the budgetary and accounting principles provided for in the General Budget Act for entities belonging to the administrative public sector. Then, that concern of the Plural Left has already been voluntarily satisfied by the aforementioned agreement between the House of His Majesty the King and the General Intervention of the State. Amendment 2644 of the Progressive Union and Democracy is still curious. He acknowledges that the budget of the House of His Majesty the King for 2016 is similar to that of 2014 and 2015 and 8% lower than in 2011. He also acknowledges the gesture of his majesty Felipe VI to reduce his salary by 20%. Well, after this background he commends, he proposes that the Royal House's budget be returned to the Government. Will it be to raise the endowment? Otherwise, after the background, there is no explanation for the whole amendment. Amendment 2857, by Esquerra Republicana, has already taken root for many years. He repeatedly proposed that the king be given a salary similar to that of the President of the Government, but did not say how all the expenses of the institution's staff, office, travel, etc. were financed. As article 65, paragraph 2, of the Constitution states that the king freely appoints and relieves the civilian and military members of his house, he seems to claim that his majesty relieves the present members and does not name anyone else. The Galego Nationalist Block, in its amendment 3322, proposes to reduce the amount allocated to His Majesty's House by 30%, under the false argument that cuts in the budgets of the Royal House were lower in previous years of economic recession and budgetary adjustment. Looking at developments over a long period from 2003 to 2013, we note that the increase in non-financial expenditure by the State during that period was 40,58 % and that the consumer price index during the same period was 27,50 %. Although it seems incredible, the budget of the House of His Majesty the King in that period has increased by 3.49% and, despite this, in the last four years it has been reduced by 13.20 %. The budgets of the House of His Majesty the King cost us every Spanish 16 cents a year. Obviously, we will vote against all the amendments to the House of His Majesty the King's budget for unjust and ungrateful. In section 02, General Courts, two amendments have been tabled by the Plural Left, which have the same concept and motivation and however different amounts. They relate to the current expenditure of the Military Life Observatory, which is a function of the Congress of Deputies. Amendment 2026 proposes EUR 60,000 and EUR 55,000 in 1996. We are going to vote against, because it is not clear that within Congress there is a budget of its own. The Progress Union and Democracy is proposing that the budget of Section 2 be returned to the Government, with a serious complaint in its statement that it does not explain. It speaks - and I quote literally - of major scandals of undue expenses. I request UPyD to give an explanation to such a grave accusation. On the other hand, no amendments have been tabled to sections 3, 4, 5 and 8. Ladies and gentlemen, this is my last speech in plenary after five legislatures remained in this House, three supporting the Government and two in the opposition. I have had the honour, not because of my merits, but because of my age, of twice presiding over the constituent session of the House. ParlaMint-ES_2015-09-16-CD150916.u95	Thank you very much, Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, this is the last intervention in this budget debate and it seems to me that we in the Commission and now in plenary have a number of points to make. The first is of a personal nature and coincides with what has been said here. First of all, I would like to congratulate the President of the Commission, because I think he has done a great deal of work by directing the hard work of the multiple votes. Those who are not members of the Commission can already imagine that the hundreds of votes we are holding here with electronic help should be made in the Commission simply on the basis of the good observation or perception and accounting capacity of the President and the Commission's lawyers. I therefore express my thanks on behalf of my group; we have been very comfortable and have been able to do the work that was appropriate. I would also like to thank the President of the House. I want to extend not only to this debate but to the whole of this Parliament our perception, which has been a good Presidency; I think it is fair to say at this time, because we are already at the end of the debate. This is a debate that leaves us all dissatisfied because we repeat the same debates in the Commission and in plenary, in addition to the same votes. In spite of everything we have a conviction, that we have not been able - this is a mea guilt that must be taken together - to improve budgets, despite all the good personal contributions of all groups without exception. I know that from the first to the last of the majority Members know that these budgets, like all human works, are imperfect; surely they can claim that they are the best that could be done, but they are imperfect. Like any human work, not to achieve perfection, but to get close to it, it is always good to listen, debate, and pay attention to something - something - what the rest of the political groups say to incorporate it. Unfortunately, with one exception, it has not happened this year either, and it is very regrettable. I am aware that many Members of the majority group regret this. We do not do things right, ladies and gentlemen, if not all the good contributions of any group are used in this House, when they are things of common sense. My group was reviewing the amendments today and we saw one that was intended to correct a purely technical, gas-denomination error. An amendment that simply wanted to be called liquefied natural gas to liquefied natural gas has not even been taken into account. Something as simple as this has also not counted on the approval of the majority; this is not good for democracy or for the majority group itself because it gives an image of not wanting to attend to anything, of wanting to possess the whole truth, of wanting to dispense from the whole world. I believe that today the citizens do not demand this from us, but rather that we are able to engage in dialogue. This is the negative thing which, sadly, has to be put on the balance sheet of the budget debate right now. Ladies and gentlemen, these are budgets that we do not know if they will enter into force. My group is convinced that on 1 January there will be a very different new majority in this House, a new majority which fortunately will no longer be absolute. We would like this new majority to be much more sensitive than the current one to citizens' problems; above all, we would like this new majority to be more sensitive to what they represent. That is to say, I can think that I have a certain quota of reason, but I do not have it all; if people are to be better cared for, the good contributions of any of the Members of the House must be accepted. I wish this to be so, I wish it to all those who may be here in the next legislature. Of course, absolute majorities are the reflection of citizenship at a given time, they are legitimate. The use of absolute majority is totally legitimate, but a reflection must be made. If someone, who has a very important absolute majority, has the expectation of losing it with so much laziness, it is something, surely, has not been done entirely well. This is the summary; surely, a sad summary. In any case, our group has tried to bring the best of itself, as we have been doing for many years. In some budgets we have been heard and in others, like this, our opinion has not been told at all, but be sure that the people of Convergence - I also dare to speak on behalf of the people of Democratic Union - will continue to bet on dialogue, because it is the only way forward. Thank you very much, Mr President. Thank you very much, ladies and gentlemen. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2015-09-16-CD150916.u97	Thank you, Mr. President. Ladies and gentlemen, today we are finalizing the General Budgets of the State in Congress, and over the course of these weeks we have heard many criticisms not only of its content, but of the very fact of its processing and approval. We have not heard a single argument - or a single argument - about what advantages it would have for the Spanish economy not to approve them. We have not heard a single explanation of what would be the benefits for the labour market and for job creation that would delay your approval for six or seven months, as you all wish, gentlemen of the opposition. Do you know why we haven't heard them? Just because there aren't. You all know that approving these budgets is good for the economy and also for employment. Ladies and gentlemen, by way of example, I cannot see the benefits that public workers would not be able to enjoy in January of their first salary increase in five years. I cannot see what benefits it would have for R & D+i not being able to renew or sign new research agreements or contracts on 1 January 2016. I cannot see them, and you have not been able in your more than sixty speeches to mention even one of those benefits that Spain would enjoy not approving these budgets. On the contrary, we know the disastrous consequences of the PSOE fleeing in 2011, when Mr Zapatero and Mr Sánchez escaped without daring to leave in writing the legacy that bequeathed us, a country on the verge of rescue, in economic ruin and unemployment, with a lot of unemployment. [[Applauds]] Spain is not for frivolities, ladies and gentlemen. Unemployed Spaniards cannot afford to lose six months and the rate of job creation is slowing down, currently above 500,000 jobs per year; in addition, quality jobs are created in all autonomous communities and in all productive sectors. Our economy cannot stop now that we have reached a rate of economic growth above 3%. This Government and this parliamentary group will continue to carry out reforms until the last day, reforms by and for the Spaniards, and will not waste a single minute of their time in making electoral calculations. [[Applauds]] We leave that to you, us to continue working out of responsibility with the Spaniards. Ladies and gentlemen, the revenue item in these budgets is a true reflection of economic recovery. It is thanks to the increased economic activity, the increase in the number of Spaniards who have a job, the greater number of companies and their higher turnover, the greater disposable income and the increase in domestic consumption; it is thanks to the greater competitiveness of our companies and the record figures of exports; it is thanks to all this that we see the increase in tax revenues planned for 2016, and this despite the fact that 80% of Spaniards today pay less IRPF than in 2011, and have already noticed it in their payrolls, their pensions and their retentions. Despite this significant tax reduction, tax revenues are increasing. This is demonstrated by the figures provided a few days ago by the Tax Agency for the first half of the year: an increase in VAT collection for SMEs by 11,2 % and also in corporate tax collection, which rises by 22 %. I also mention the IRPF, with a 16% increase in split professional payments in the first half of this year. Please note that I have spoken about SMEs and self-employed people, who account for 90% of our productive fabric. This means that today we have more economic activity and more employment. In short, we're recovering. Mr Saura, listening to you or Mr Sánchez criticising this government's income policy is a real sarcasm. You were here actively participating in the economic policy of the previous government, when you had the dubious honor of having achieved the worst results in almost a hundred years, with a fall of 70 billion euros in the collection. [[Applauds]] And now, they present a series of amendments to - they say - increase the collection through recipes that have little or nothing new and that coincide in no way casually with those proposed by the populist parties with which they not only share municipalities and autonomous communities, but also - apparently - economic recipes. It is frankly disturbing to see how Mr Sánchez endorses and shares the economic prescriptions of the extreme left, an economic policy that experience in other countries and in our environment shows us that they are useless, harmful and very painful for the citizens, which is why all analysts and all national and international institutions consider that the greatest danger to the Spanish economy is political instability and the return to the approved reforms, exactly what you - the Socialist Party - represent and their pacts with the extreme left. You are the greatest danger to the economy and to employment, because danger, ladies and gentlemen, is written with pe, with the pe of Peter, with the pe of Paul, with the pe of Podemos, with the pe of PSOE; in short, with the pe of populism. [[Applauds]] [[Applauds.- Several Members speak words that are not perceived.]] I shall now finish, Mr President, but first of all I would like to thank the Bureau of the Committee on Budgets, the lawyers, the members of the paper and, in particular, my colleagues, Matilde Asian González and Vicente Martínez-Pujalte, to whom we wish the best of luck in the future. The People's Parliamentary Group will certainly miss its magnificent work and even more its great human quality. [[Applauds]] Before I finish, I would like to address the Government. In Spain there has been a great change over the past four years: from being on the verge of rescue and being the greatest danger for the euro and even for the European Union itself, to being the fastest growing and most employment-creating country in our environment. This change is mainly the result of the efforts of the Spanish, but also - and it is worth acknowledging - of the reforms promoted by your Government, Mr President. In this regard, I sincerely believe - and I say this to you on behalf of the whole People's Parliamentary Group - that you can be legitimately proud, for you have done a great service to this country. Thank you very much, Mr President. Thank you very much, ladies and gentlemen, for your attention. [[Prolonged applause]]

Governments change, majorities change, but discrimination against Valencians remains. Maybe we haven't been clear enough. We must understand that in the marginalization we suffer rains on wet and injustices accumulate. We talk about autonomous financing, an outdated model; we are poor and we pay as rich, an anomaly that only happens in colonial regimes. We talk about debt, 15,315 million historical debt since 2002. We're talking about investments. How can they explain to the Valencians that we Valencians have had less than average investment and our population weight continuously and systematically? As I was saying, in this matter it rains on wet, and the Valencians know what happens when it rains on wet, both in the literal sense and in the figurative sense: nothing good. So far has come the patience of those who are treated year after year as second-rate citizens. And it is that the Valencians and the Valencians did not find any explanation for the discrimination. Aren't they people who use underfunded public health and education? Or the lack of Gandia to Denia, the most studied route in history? Or those who die constantly on the N-340 road in the absence of alternatives between Castellón and Vinaròs? Aren't they people who see how their inner environment loses potential due to a lack of communication like the Xàtiva-Alcoy axis? Ladies and gentlemen, all these people need no more promises, they need fair investments. If today finally there is a vote in favour of this reform, Congress will be recognizing the injustice of insufficient investment in our territory. But that recognition must be accompanied by facts, solutions, and that is where we come to the problem. You may think that we come here looking for a pat on the back, that our goal is to return home with the satisfaction that you have heard our protests. Do not be mistaken, we come to tell you that the Valencians have tired of paying and silence; we come to tell you that our patience has a limit; we come to tell you that when politicians shut up, people speak. Listen to the Valencian people and act accordingly. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

We just want not to be unequal among equals in this complex world, which is that of the autonomous state, in which each of us wants the best for our peoples, we want to be the same as the rest of the Spaniards. ParlaMint-ES_2015-09-16-CD150916.u322	Very late to all and to all and a special greeting to the representatives of the Valencian Courts in the name of the Republicanism of the Catalan Countries. We finally discussed the proposal for the reform of the Statute of Valencian Autonomy that the Valencian Corts sent in 2011. The absolute majority of the People's Party in Les Corts has imposed four years of postponements for this debate. Obviously, a debate more interested in using financing as a bold weapon when in Madrid they did not send their own than in resolving the lack of financing and investment for the Valencia Country. Let us not be fooled, this reform is only a symbolic gesture, it gives no solution to the Spanish Government's lack of investment in the Valencia Country. This is, in fact, acknowledged by the statement of reasons itself when it states that the Constitutional Court, in its judgment on the Statute of Catalonia, states that the provisions of the Spanish Government's investment regulations do not bind the General Courts in drawing up the State's budgets. In other words, as much as the reform of the Statut d'Autonomia says that the investment of the State in the Valencia Country must be equivalent to the weight of its population in the total of the State, the Government will be able to ignore, will be able to literally do whatever it wants. However, this symbolic gesture must allow an important thing, which is to visualize an endemic problem. In the last fifteen General Budgets of the State, the per capita investment planned for the Valencià Country has always been below the average of the whole State. It is not punctual, it is repeated systematically every year, regardless of who is in La Moncloa and the Generalitat. If the population criterion laid down in the proposed reform since 2000 had been applied, the Spanish Government should have invested more than EUR 6.8 billion; EUR 6.8 billion could have been used to finance, for example, the works of the Mediterranean corridor, the García-Denia connection, the Xàtiva-Alcoi line, the unfolding of the N-332 as it passes through the Navy or the N-340 by the Baix Maestrat, for a few examples. We would have liked those who are voting in favour of this reform today to have been consistent and to have voted in favour of the amendments tabled by Esquerra Republicana for more than a decade; some amendments to finance the projects referred to and which would have made it possible to alleviate the discrimination that Valencià has suffered in the field of investment. State investment is only the tip of the iceberg of the Valencian financing problem, ladies and gentlemen, which is a unique case in the world. It is a territory with a per capita income below the average, which nevertheless provides the State with more taxes than it then receives in terms of spending and investment. This fact is confirmed even by the Minister of Finance himself, Mr Montoro, who showed a fiscal deficit of more than EUR 1.4 billion with the publication of his famous fiscal balances. It is therefore intolerable that the Valencia Country should be forced to contribute more than it receives when the funding system does not even cover expenditure on health and education. And even worse, instead of modifying the financing mechanism so that the taxes paid by Valencians can finance their basic services, the Spanish Government offers only loans. All this while forcing itself to incur a real deficit as long as the problem of financing is not solved. All this problem was also exposed by Esquerra Republicana during the reform of the Statut d'Autonomia in 2006, when we demanded that the financing be fixed in a bilateral commission based on the expenditure needs of the Generalitat and that a Valencian tax agency be set up to manage the collection of all taxes borne by Valencians and Valencians. Our amendments, of course, were also rejected by the People's Party and the Spanish Socialist Workers' Party. The Valencians cannot wait any longer; this continued outburst has impoverished them and prevented them from having the resources to deal with the economic crisis and, of course, the social crisis with guarantees. The Valencià Country does not need the crumbs of an improvement of the investment, ladies and gentlemen, but it rightly demands - it claims, I repeat - a change in the financing that allows it to develop the social policies and the economic and investment policies that it needs. I'm done right at five minutes. As Ovidi Montllor said: they no longer feed us soft, now we want pa senser. Thank you very much, Madam President.

ParlaMint-ES_2015-09-16-CD150916.u324	Thank you very much, Madam President. I welcome the delegation of representatives of Les Corts. Having said that, I don't think we're going to surprise anyone if we say that in Spain we have a system of financing the disabled and unequal autonomous communities, which allocates investments in a differentiated way depending on one territory or another. What causes this? That there are people who by chance have been born in one autonomous community or another have greater resources than others. Thus, according to official data, the non-financial resources per inhabitant mean that the average per inhabitant living in autonomous communities of common system amounts to 2,763 euros, while the average per inhabitant living in communities with special regime - Basque Country and Navarre - amounts to 4,829 euros. My parliamentary group, UPyD, is totally against this type of inequality and totally in favour of a system of equitable financing for all Spaniards. However, we need to take into account or ask ourselves a number of questions before making decisions that, far from solving these specific problems, end up further complicating the situation, generating expectations that will be frustrated, unsatisfied, or generating alterations that are difficult to solve a posteriori in the financing system. To argue that because a number of autonomous communities, such as Catalonia or Andalusia, have included in their statutes additional provisions to ensure a permanent and stable volume of State investments in their respective territories, since the Valencian Community did not at the time wish to include in the new Statute clauses or forecasts that could unduly condition the State in the exercise of its powers and that therefore could be dubiously compatible with the Constitution, is not sufficient reason to request its amendment now. It seems to us to be a somewhat childish argument, inconsistent and respectful with the Valencians. On the other hand, if we take into account the legal basis 138 of the Constitutional Court's judgment of 28 June 2010 on the Statute of Autonomy of Catalonia, a modification such as that which is intended does not bind these General Courts in the exercise of their functions of examination, amendment and approval of the budgets, since the financial commitments formalized in the Statutes of Autonomy do not constitute a remedy which the State must make compulsory in the general budgets of each financial year. So, this change that we bring today would be a toast to the sun, a theoretical instrument that would not solve the problem for the rest of the Spaniards but not for the Valencians. Most new statutes and reform projects include seemingly innocuous clauses that condition State investments in each autonomous community. Although in reality it does not directly affect financing, it is again a unconstitutional invasion of state powers by these autonomous communities, which would also prevent in practice the simultaneous application of all of them, since these clauses allow the most favourable variable for each autonomous community, which defines it as if it were: weight on GDP, population, area, population dispersion, etc., so that the sum of what the State should invest in each would be greater than the total investment defined by the State, paying direct attention to what is supposed to be a federal State. These reforms, promoted by the PP and the PSOE at the autonomous level and endorsed by the PP and the PSOE on many occasions at the national level, involve a false system of bilateral negotiation of autonomous financing, purporting to elevate the autonomous communities as equal before the State. As has been said on several occasions in my parliamentary group, the President of the Government is no longer merely a coordinator of seventeen autonomous communities; now he is not even expected to coordinate, but they are seeking to negotiate in some way equally with the autonomous communities. Adopting this amendment, which would imply a sort of Autonomy Statute 2.0, would end the responsibility and fiscal equality for all Spaniards, widening the gap of inequality. In addition, we would launch completely confused messages and headlines for the electorate that would not solve the problem. Therefore, from our group we ask and come to demand an in-depth reform, an analysis of inequality: what produces it and the problems generated by the current autonomous system promoted by both the PP and the PSOE. We propose fiscal unity for Spain - as we defend it for Europe - to ensure equality between all Spaniards, regardless of the territory in which they are located. Therefore, we cannot do anything other than vote against the proposal to reform the first additional provision of the Statute of Autonomy of the Valencian Community in the terms in which it reaches us today. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2015-09-16-CD150916.u340	Points III and IV of the agenda, concerning the consideration of the proposed law of the People's Group in the Organic Congress, amending the Organic Law 2/1979 of 3 October of the Constitutional Court, for the implementation of the resolutions of the Constitutional Court as a guarantee of the rule of law, and the agreement for the direct and single reading of this initiative, which are processed cumulatively, in accordance with what we have agreed on in the Board of Spokesmen. ParlaMint-ES_2015-09-16-CD150916.u341	Thank you, Mr. President. Ladies and gentlemen, the 1978 Constitution is the essential standard of coexistence which, drawn up by political consensus and backed by overwhelming social support, has been the greatest success in terms of the development, prosperity and freedoms of democratic Spain and since we have had historical memory. In addition, it has been a constitution that has served as a model and example for many others in Latin America and Eastern European countries who wanted to copy the transition to democracy from the Spanish model; an example of success and progress from peace and democratic pluralism. Our constitution is one of the most modern in Europe not only because it is one of the most recent in the continent, but because it knows how to bind modernity and tradition, since it is a norm that entangles its roots in the liberal Constitution of 1812 and that, encompassing elements of the one of 1931, manages to look in the mirror of the most advanced constitutions of Europe, such as the Italian and the German ones. Our framework of coexistence, based on the Constitution, has allowed for a political alternation in which minorities, whether nationalistic or not, have played a very important role, participating in the governance of this country regardless of whether it is a left or centre-right government. Spain is among the most decentralized countries in the world and this model of decentralization, which is not based on federalism or regionalism and yes on the affirmation of the state of autonomy, has been a very important milestone for its contribution to the involvement of the various territories, nationalities and regions of article 2 of the Constitution, and has been essential in the recovery and configuration of democracy of the nascent Spanish political regime of the Transition, overcoming the centralist system in force until then in Spain. If the Constitution of 1812 was known as the Mestiza, the Constitution of 1978 is the Constitution of the Agreement, because thanks to the synthesis of features it contains in its drafting it has promoted and enhanced this framework of coexistence that has made the Spaniards during the last almost forty years and regardless of the place of origin we have felt citizens and participants in this great common project that is the Spain of the twenty-first century, a vertebrate and European Spain. Therefore, talking about a reform of the same can only be asked if it is from agreement and consensus, seeking exclusively to improve our coexistence, looking towards the future and not as a way out of the internal problems of any political formation seeking to recover recipes that have already failed in the past. The Constitution guarantees the rule of law as an expression of popular will and this legality is based on national sovereignty, which resides in the Spanish people, a concept that is neither fractionable nor divisible. That is why it is not acceptable for a few people to try to decide what belongs to us all, and that is because they would be deciding what does not belong to them, taking possession of a power that is not attributable to them, because sovereignty, ladies and gentlemen, is intangible and indivisible. Either you wear it and you have it or you just don't have it, but you can't plot it, you can't split it, you can't cut it, you can't cut it, you can't tear it apart, you know, like some people claim. The Constitution, therefore, is the cornerstone of our rule of law, which underpins the indissoluble unity of the Spanish nation, the common homeland of all Spaniards; it guarantees the full autonomy of nationalities and regions and the solidarity between them, which must serve precisely to rebalance and to improve and reduce inequalities between them. In order to protect the Constitution, a constitutional justice system was adopted, with a Constitutional Court acting as an interpreter and defender of the Constitution, as well as guaranteeing the fundamental rights of the Spaniards. This legislation, which is the law we intend to amend today, has already been reformed by this House on a total of eight occasions. For example, Organic Law 5/1985, which repealed part of this same law through a proposal for a law of the Socialist Party, eliminates the previous remedy of unconstitutionality for organic laws and draft statutes of autonomy. Also the Organic Law 7/1999, which in turn amended the law so that the Constitutional Court could hear conflicts in defence of local autonomy. Or Organic Law 6/2007, which established a new regulation for the admission of amparo. And we have recently adopted in this House, ladies and gentlemen, another amendment promoting the reintroduction of the previous remedy of unconstitutionality for projects and proposals for reform of autonomous statutes by means of two proposals for legislation, one of which was promoted by the People's Parliamentary Group and the other by the Socialist Parliamentary Group. Therefore, ladies and gentlemen, this is not the first time that this law has been amended, nor is it the first time that it has been amended by a bill tabled by a parliamentary group in this House. Reforms on three of these occasions were made through legislative proposals, two by the Socialist Group. It is true that it would have been desirable for some of them, such as 1985, to have avoided it; thus the previous recourse for unconstitutionality in matters relating to the statutes of autonomy would not have been eliminated and we would have saved ourselves many of the muds that are coming today. In any case, ladies and gentlemen, the reform contained in this initiative is consistent with the spirit of the law and will, above all, serve to bring the Constitutional Court into line with the shortcomings felt in recent years, giving it a number of powers in order to enforce its judgments, powers which it has so far lacked. Moreover, it is the reality that has shown us that the degree of non-compliance and disregard for its resolutions has gradually increased and, why not say so, this situation has occurred almost simultaneously with the development of the independence movement created in Catalonia, whose sole objective is to destroy Spain, but also that of other new parties that have recently arrived at the institutions and that have as leitmotiv the one that says that only the laws that one likes must be complied with. Thus, when the Constitutional Court, ladies and gentlemen, requests in a judgment a positive action by a public authority and the latter responds with a conscious inactivity, there is non-compliance and contempt for its explicit refusal to comply with the provisions, either through the fraudulent omission of what is required or simply refusing to proceed with the repeal of the law or autonomous acts reprobated by the Constitutional Court. The cases are numerous and evident. First of all, the ruling on the Statute of Catalonia's Autonomy, which is paradigmatic in relation to the predominant use of Catalan and the exclusion of Castilian as a vehicle language also in the territory of education, has led many parents to renounce their right to school their children in Castilian because of the pressure exerted every day by exclusionary and virulent nationalism that we have learned today in some newspapers that are blushing with shame. And other cases are the Catalan Veguerías Act, the suspension of the call for a referendum by the Generalitat on 9 September 2014 or the challenge of the National Transition Commissioner. Similarly, on 11 June last the Constitutional Court declared the unconstitutionality of the so-called process of citizen participation and this very week the Constitutional Court ordered the suspension of the planned allocation of resources that the Generalitat had agreed for the Catalan Tax Agency, while the Vice-President of the Generalitat, Mrs. Munté - take note of this, Mr. Hernando - said yesterday that she will maintain her plan to summon the necessary places in a clear disregard of the ruling of the Constitutional Court. In all these cases the Generalitat de Catalunya has put and is showing a single attitude: non-compliance as a response, contempt as conduct and institutional disloyalty as a flag. And all this to endorse a purported national affirmation based on the immoral victimism of false aggravation. Moreover, these contempts and non-compliances not only affect Catalonia, but they have transcendence for the rest of Spain; they have an impact on the equality of the Spanish before the law - not only of the Catalan citizens themselves, but of all others - and ultimately cannot be tolerated from the perspective of a rule of law that can be described as such, and of course they cannot and will not be tolerated by a Government that wants to maintain and defend the integrity of Spain and ensure respect for the principles that provide stability in the framework of coexistence between us by defending the principle of legality and equality of all citizens before the law. [[Applauds]] Ladies and gentlemen, there is no doubt that the dissociation between the inability of the court to enforce the provisions of its judgments and the relevant political depth burden of many of its resolutions has led to an inconsistency which has not helped to strengthen the effectiveness of its judgments. For this reason we have brought this initiative to the House, taking into account the regulation in some countries of our environment, especially in the cases of Germany or Austria, where the Constitutional Court can explicitly determine in its judgments the most appropriate method of enforcement; or Italy, where the Constitutional Court can even impose a criminal sanction on those who have violated the Italian Constitution; or France, where the Constitutional Council has important enforcement powers. Following closely the models of comparative law cited above, we have articulated a proposal for a law that amends four articles of the Organic Law on the Functioning of the Constitutional Court with the aim of ensuring the full implementation of its judgments and rulings. Ladies and gentlemen, the proposed measures and the mechanisms established are grouped into four parts. The first refers to assistance in the enforcement of court decisions by administrations and public authorities. The Constitutional Court may request the assistance of any government or public authority. The reform explicitly states that the decisions of the Constitutional Court will have the consideration of executive titles. This is a step forward with regard to the current situation, since giving this character to the rulings of the Constitutional Court will make a decisive contribution to facilitating the enforcement of its rulings, as is the case with the rest of the final rulings of our court order. The second part concerns personal notification to public authorities and employees. So far, ladies and gentlemen, the absence of a specific notifiable addressee made it more complex to demand compliance with the judgments. As a result of this reform, it ended up engaging in flags or collective responsibilities so as not to face criminal responsibilities. The third part specifically regulates the powers of the court. In the event of failure to comply with the resolution, the reform provides the court with the capacity to adopt some of the following coercive measures, which are similar to those already provided for in the administrative and litigation order. First, to impose a coercive fine on a person who fails to comply with the judgment; that is, no one will be released from contempt of the Constitutional Court's judgements for free. [[Applauds]] Secondly, to agree on the suspension in their duties of the authorities or public employees responsible for non-compliance. Therefore, ladies and gentlemen, this is a reasoned decision on provisional and temporary suspension, not a disqualification, but which will certainly lead to the enforcement of judgments even if some do not want to. Thirdly, to agree on the replacement execution by the Government of the nation. And fourthly, to deduce the appropriate testimony to demand the criminal liability that may be incurred, which seems to be a logical correlation of the monetary fine. The fourth part of our bill, ladies and gentlemen, provides for urgent action to take the necessary measures to implement it. Its purpose is to prevent the passage of time from frustrating or hindering the proper execution of court judgements. Given the content of the proposal, the material opportunity of the reform, which is a simple reform and would require little clarification, is clearly being observed, but I will focus on the issue because of the sudden and surprising criticisms of the opposition to the process of this initiative. According to its authors - Mr Hernando has just told us - its objective is to extend the powers available to the Constitutional Court to enforce its judgments. In particular, they wish to entrust the court with a new legal duty, that of ensuring ex officio and at the request of the party for the effective implementation of their decisions, adopting the necessary enforcement measures or seeking the help of other authorities. What is new is that they have decided that the court can make a substitute execution of their decisions or even agree to suspend the functions of authorities and officials. On 11 August 1980 the Constitutional Court delivered its first judgement. Since then there have been thousands - last year 215 and 305 cars - and so far no one has raised the need for the reform that you bring here today, because everyone understands that the court has sufficient instruments to enforce its sentences. No constitutionalist, no institution has missed what you propose today, over these thirty-five years. Furthermore, do you want to tell me that all the groups in the House, the 350 Members, for thirty-five years we have been irresponsible because we have not proposed this which you bring here today? [[Applauds]] No, ladies, no, we haven't been irresponsible. The truth is, the only truth is that the only objective of this initiative is to hold a rally, a rally in the Congress of Deputies. The protagonist of this rally is Mr. Albiol and the banner of it is: The joke is over. This is the objective of this initiative. Look, neither in the constitutional debates, nor in the debates on reforming the Constitutional Court Act was the need for this initiative ever raised, nor in the proposals that have been presented here over thirty-five years. In the most classic work of constitutionalism of 1978, which is Commentary to the Constitution, directed by Óscar Alzaga, we dedicate Volume XII to the Constitutional Court, more than 400 pages dedicated to the Constitutional Court, not a single mention of the need to give an added competence of this nature to the Constitutional Court, but not even a recommendation of lege ferenda. Under no circumstances does any constitutionalist, until now - let us see if Mr Hernando can now convince us otherwise - and that is because since the adoption of his Organic Law section 92 has been sufficient. The court has the power to order in its judgement or in subsequent acts who has to execute it and, where appropriate, to resolve the consequences of the execution in the event of non-compliance or non-compliance with the decision. It has the power to declare null and void decisions which contravene decisions handed down in the exercise of its jurisdiction or on the occasion of the execution of such decisions. What this proposal envisages, therefore, is not to give the court the means to comply with its judgments - those already have them - but to extend its powers, and that is really what you want to do, to extend the powers of the Constitutional Court by granting it a sanctioning power of a different nature from the sanctioning power it currently has, and with this what you actually achieve is what you want: to change the legal nature of the Constitutional Court and to make it different from what our constituent envisaged for it. Some representatives of the Government and the People's Party - today we have seen it - say that this capacity is already held by other constitutional courts in our environment, for example - said Mr Hernando - the Austrian or the German. Yes, ladies and gentlemen, yes, they do, of course, but it is that in the Austrian Constitution and in the German Constitution this is provided for, while in ours that is not foreseen, and that is the question, that these constitutional courts were designed in that sense in their respective constitutions. For example, the German Constitutional Court, which is also part of the judiciary in accordance with its Basic Law, can separate the Federal President from office before the Federal Constitutional Court for serious and wilful breach of the Basic Law, certainly at the request of the Bundesrat and the Bundestag - stay with this because it is important for what I will then say - and has many other powers that have nothing to do with those of the Spanish Constitutional Court despite the fact that the Spanish Constitutional Court, as you know, does take its spirit from the German Constitutional Court. The Austrian Constitutional Court has other powers that have nothing to do with it, but also because it says so in its own Constitution: it knows of the claims of property responsibility, of the objections of the election of federal president, of the regional governments and of the municipal bodies and even of the deprivation of the minutes of the deputy. Something to do with the Spanish Constitutional Court? No, nothing, Mr. Hernando. Therefore, I am not worthy of the example of the Austrian Constitutional Court or the German Constitutional Court. The Spanish Constitution does not attribute this to the Constitutional Court surely because the parents of the Constitution had a sad memory of what had been the Court of Guarantees of the Republic. [[Applauds]] After thirty-five years of Constitutional Court, do you really believe that a single party can change the institutional architecture provided for in our Constitution in thirty days? Ladies and gentlemen, redefining at this stage of the legislature the legal nature of the Constitutional Court, Minister, is not an irresponsibility, it is a danger. With the existing model, the court exercises its functions as an interpreter of the constitutional text, taking account exclusively of the parameter of jurisdiction, even being in a moderating, inclusive, quasi-arbitrary position. The court, in order to resolve conflicts of jurisdiction between the State and the autonomous communities, already has the power to enforce judgements and decisions. If we've seen it forty-eight hours ago! We've seen it! And it hasn't been through an execution procedure. It has been through a simple measure: Article 4 of the Tax Agency Act approved by the Catalan Parliament has been suspended. Our constitutional model, unless you want to change the Constitution in this sense, does not allow our Constitutional Court to have the same powers as the Austrian Constitutional Court and the German Constitutional Court, to be put on the same footing. You want to solve by disfigurement of the Constitutional Court a problem that does not belong to the field of procedural enforcement of judgments, but to the much more intricate, irregular and political conflicts between institutions. We have varied historical backgrounds in Spain and outside Spain that demonstrate the desirability of resolving certain competitive confrontations between institutions in the appropriate field and not through the punitive enforcement of sentences made by constitutional courts. There is a recent ruling from the Constitutional Court - you mentioned it, Mr Hernando -. It determines the unconstitutionality of the declaration of the Parliament of Catalonia on sovereignty and the right to decide by the people of Catalonia and establishes in its legal basis 4.(b) the following: 'The Constitution does not address or can expressly address all the problems that may arise in the constitutional order, in particular those arising from the will of a part of the State to alter its legal status. Such problems cannot be resolved by this court, whose function is to ensure strict compliance with the Constitution.' And the Constitutional follows: 'Therefore, the public authorities and, in particular, the territorial powers that make up our autonomous state are those who are called to solve the problems that arise in this area through dialogue and cooperation'. Look at the paradoxes. The Constitutional Court gives reason to the Government when it appeals, declares unconstitutionality, but in order to resolve the substantive problem it recommends dialogue, which is what has been lacking all this time, ladies and gentlemen. [[Applauds]] Ladies and gentlemen, do not disfigure the nature of the Constitutional Court, do not put it under unbearable pressure. No one has asked from the doctrine for a reform of these characteristics. No mention was made of article 92. However, they all agreed on the need for the Constitutional Court to be consulted before a hypothetical reform of the Organic Law of the Constitutional Court, taking into account its initiative and opinion. And I ask myself, listen, have you spoken to the Constitutional Court before you presented this? Because according to the media it turns out that the Constitutional Court has learned about this through the media, through the newspapers. It would be irritating, illusory if you had consulted the Constitutional Court with a reform of the law affecting the Constitutional Court. It's too much to ask, I understand. [[Applauds]] But this reform, if it is to be carried out in these terms, will also cause enormous problems of interpretation and implementation. From a technical point of view, it's very bad, honestly I'm telling you. We've heard they've been working on it for eight months. Hey, to take eight months working on it, what's gotten into them, huh? Very little. Besides, how come they've been working on it for eight months? And when two months ago we reformed the Organic Law of the Constitutional Court to recover the previous appeal that Mr. Hernando spoke of, why did they hide it from us? They've been in this for eight months, and they've been hiding them from us? They tricked us then or they fooled us now by saying that it's been eight months since they improvised it in eight days before the election. What are we left with, gentlemen? [[Applause.- Various Members: Very well!]] In any case, this is an improvisation, yes, Your Honor, it is clear. It is so improvised that on the same day and half an hour after recording it they had to put in a correction of errors because it had a mistake, but that is not the most serious thing, ladies and gentlemen, I recognize that that is not the most serious thing. The problem is not just a lack of legislative technique. The fundamental issue is one of substance. Look, with the literality of what is provided for in 92.4(b), which seems to me to be fundamental, the Constitutional Court could from now on suspend the functions of the president of an autonomous community or the president of the Government if it considers that there is even a partial breach of a ruling of the Constitutional Court. You've understood what I've told you, haven't you? To suspend the functions of a community president or the President of the Government himself. Good. You want to give the Constitutional Court a real power to suspend the President of the Government without impeachment and without previous censorship of the General Courts. Do you realize what you're doing, ladies and gentlemen? Do you know how serious this is? It is impossible to combine what you want to do in this law with our constitutional system, because the President of the Government is appointed and elected through the investiture in the General Courts and ceases through the motion of censure in the General Courts. It is therefore not possible to do so through a decision of the Constitutional Court. [[Applauds]] This is the barbarity they're doing. Too many questions and no clarification from Mr Hernando. Who can review these Constitutional Court decisions? They don't say that. What remedies can the suspended authority make in the exercise of its functions and to whom? They don't know, when would the Constitutional Court make these decisions? It doesn't count. How would the Constitutional Court choose between the different possibilities set out in article 92? It's not contemplating. The project is so technically flawed that it does not determine the circumstances in which it must apply one or other enforcement measure of article 92. And how does the Constitutional Court work, let's see? For reasons of opportunity? Like it's a purely political organ? Do you really want the Constitutional Court to act that way? In 2002, the president of the Constitutional Court, Don Manuel Jiménez de Parga, wrote an article in the ABC on the occasion that some were asking the Constitutional Court to make it possible for that body to illegalize political parties. Jiménez de Parga said: It is not possible for an organic law to assign to the Constitutional Court powers that the Constitution itself has expressly conferred on other constitutional bodies. Nor will it be possible for constitutional jurisdiction to be denaturalized in this way. Professor Jiménez de Parga continues by saying: The first objection that can be raised is that it is such a radical innovation that it entails and would seriously affect the nature of constitutional jurisdiction, as designed in the Constitution. The principle of exclusive jurisdiction under article 117.3 of the Spanish Constitution would be violated, since the power to try and enforce the case was vested only in the judiciary, so that the intervention of the Constitutional Court could only be made on the basis of a preliminary judicial ruling and always on a subsidiary basis. Everything Jiménez de Parga said in 2002 about that possibility of granting those powers to the Constitutional Court is now applicable to what you want to do with this reform. Ladies and gentlemen, we do not have this sitting today because there is a widespread conviction that the rulings of the Constitutional Court are not being complied with. That is not the case, nor can it be by the very nature of the decisions of the Constitutional Court, that when it makes a judgment declaring unconstitutional that rule automatically comes out of the legal system. That's what Kelsen called negative legislation. There is nothing more executive than a ruling by the Constitutional Court. Their judgements are fully enforceable without the need for an enforcement procedure. So don't fool anyone. This proposal has only one objective: to scratch votes by taking advantage of the independence challenge of some political formations and authorities in Catalonia. They want to use 27 September and what can happen later for their own electoral interests and only for their electoral interests. In this regard, this initiative is similar to the one of 2003. It will be remembered by some who were here as early as 2003. You in 2003 and just a month before the Chambers were dissolved, wanted to reform through the Senate Arbitration Act the Criminal Code to put the president of an autonomous community who illegally convened a referendum in prison. Do you remember? That rule which they promoted in the pre-election campaign against the lehendakari Ibarretxe was declared unconstitutional following an appeal from my group, but had previously been repealed by a socialist government. If this one goes on, it'll happen exactly the same. [[Applauds]] Now, ladies and gentlemen, you want to repeat the strategy, you want to do something very serious again in Constitutional Law, very serious in Criminal Law, which is an ad hoc rule. Nothing more ad hoc than the speech made by Mr Hernando in this rostrum, without being conscious, or being conscious, but importing them very little, that the tension to which they are going to submit to the institutions, to the Constitutional Court, is terrible. It is harmful to the rule of law, to the State and to democracy itself. Furthermore, ladies and gentlemen, I am absolutely convinced that what you are claiming is absolutely inefficient to solve the fundamental problem, as inefficient as the political inaction of the Government for almost four years. On the contrary, the only effect that the adoption of this law can have is the strengthening of the independenceist positions in Catalonia. The problem of coexistence between Catalonia and the rest of Spain and the crisis generated by the current Government of the Generalitat with actions that seek to undermine the law and that are therefore incompatible with democracy has a political background wrapped in resolutions of undoubted legal significance, but these resolutions of undoubted legal significance can already be short-circuited by the Constitutional Court. He's doing it, as we've seen, but you don't settle for it. You want to force the role of the Constitutional Court by changing its nature and that is incompatible with the Spanish Constitution. If you don't reform it, you can't do this, ladies and gentlemen. The United States Supreme Court has a doctrine, which is that of political issues, those issues on which the court decided not to rule because they constituted pure political disputes, conflicts that cannot be resolved simply by application of legal rules, but, on the contrary, their jurisdictional settlement would contribute nothing to the political settlement and could cause serious harm to justice. It was done by the United States Supreme Court to preserve the institutionality of the court itself. What can be achieved with this law and with this reform, if it goes forward, is precisely the opposite with our Constitutional Court. That is why Rubio Llorente said in these days that this law could place on the Constitutional Court a political burden that will eventually crush it. The only truth of this initiative, the ultimate reason for it, in addition to being electoral, highlights the political passivity of a president of the Government who, unable to propose solutions to a serious crisis of coexistence, decides to act at the last minute to unload his incompetence on the backs of the Constitutional Court and hide behind it. A constitutional criminal jurisdiction, that's what they're asking us.
