It is an absolute denaturalization of the Constitutional Court, a nonsense, and then it is also a sluggish one. Following the same argument, some decisions of the Constitutional Court have been cited, and I would like to quote one very recently. Do you remember the debate that took place precisely in 2013 as a result of the membership of the President of the Constitutional Court in the government party? The Constitutional Court issued an order recalling that the Constitutional Court - intercommissioned - is a State institution which, like its judges, is not part of the judiciary, cannot act as a criminal jurisdiction. It is impossible, even if they want to attribute it in this reform of the Organic Law of the Constitutional Court, it is absolutely impossible. The Constitutional Court has not only declaratory instruments. Mr. Esteban, they amended article 92 of the Constitutional Court itself by introducing a new paragraph so that, in addition to the statement, it was established how a decision of the Constitutional Court could be enforced that those who had an obligation to execute it did not want to do so. They amended article 92 by adding a new paragraph in which the Constitutional Court could identify exactly the persons who had to comply with that resolution and, if they did not comply with it, it was when criminal jurisdiction began to take effect; criminal jurisdiction began to take action against crimes that already existed, for example, sedition. I'm not inventing it, it's in the Penal Code and the penal codes of all the democracies of the world. Therefore, we go to the next action, after the proceedings already held by the Constitutional Court to request and declare the nullity or suspension of a unilateral declaration, in this case, of independence. It has the power to issue declaratory resolutions and also to issue subsequent acts. There are also legal solutions in the case of conscious and deliberate non-compliance with resolutions; they exist, but you have not implemented them. For example, public demonstration of unilateral declaration of independence. Does the Constitutional Court have to act? Perhaps later it can act, but a public demonstration of unilateral declaration of independence - let us stand on the night of 27 or the morning of 28 - is a relevant sign of the crime of sedition, as established in our Penal Code, but it is the criminal court that has to act in this situation. In that case, the entry into prison of the persons concerned would not be ruled out, even before the Constitutional Court had ruled on the matter. It is simply a matter of activating the prosecutor's office and also the Attorney General's Office and telling them to act. Similarly, if they refused to comply with the mandate, due diligence, state attorney and prosecutor's office, possible crimes of prevarication and disobedience. It is in our legal order, but you must instruct the State Attorney General and the lawyer to act, without perverting the Constitutional Court, without perverting the work of the Constitutional Court or the very essence of the Constitutional Court. Finally, there is the so-called article 155 of the Constitution. The Constitutional Court - I have told you the instruments -, criminal jurisdiction - I have cited some examples, some instruments - and then comes 155 of the Constitution. People with little suspicion of not knowing the law and little suspicion of not wanting to interpret it well have endeavoured to call article 155 of the Constitution the suspensory article of autonomy, and I doubt it permanently. I would like people to read it to them, because 155 is an ordinary article of the Constitution, it is not an extraordinary article, it is not intended for Catalonia or for the Basque Country, it has been in the Constitution since it was adopted. It is an ordinary article of the Constitution that is not to punish anyone but to defend the general interest, the rule of law, so that the law is enforced on an equal basis in any territory of Spain, to protect the citizens of a ruler who becomes crazy - crazy in terms of not wanting to apply the rulings of the courts or the Constitutional Court - of a despotic ruler. For this is article 155, so that it is complied with equally in any part of Spain, so that the rule of law is guaranteed. It is an article to protect the Spaniards, to protect the citizens, to protect the general interest. It is an article that, with a different title and a different number, exists in any democratic Constitution of the world, because it is the defense of the general interest and the rule of law that must prevail in any democracy of the world. Therefore, if the first thing fails and the second thing fails, that is, if the instruments I mentioned above fail, or if they do not absolutely fail and fail relatively, there is also the application of the 155, which has to be activated by the Government without any complexity, because it has not only the obligation but also the responsibility to act in this way and in this matter of article 97 of the Constitution, which says that what it has to do is precisely that, the defence of the State. It says: it is up to the government to defend the state. And for that it has many instruments, and one neither minor nor greater nor extraordinary is article 155 of the Constitution.

We all know that the statute of limitations set out in article 66 of the General Tax Act is a general statute of limitations, which refers to the four-year period. The statute of limitations is an institution that serves legal certainty and is now supplemented in this draft law by the amendment introduced in articles 115 and 66 bis of the draft. I said it and I repeat it: This amendment must be interpreted restrictively, sensu stricto, without making academic nuance between the right to liquidate and the right to verify. Only the Administration may verify compensated or outstanding bases or quotas or deductions applied or outstanding within a maximum period of 10 years, provided that they have effect in unprescribed periods. Secondly, the objective of this entire regulatory battery is to recover tax collection. The 2007 crisis caused the collection to fall from 2007 to 2009 by EUR 60 billion, putting the welfare state in grave danger. Law 16/2012, on Tax Measures for the Consolidation of Public Finances, contained, it is true, tax measures contrary to our political DNA, which is to lower taxes, but which were necessary to prevent the bankruptcy of the State and which we have been able to reverse in the last year. Thirdly, the aim was to combat tax fraud. Law 7/2012, called the Anti-Fraud Act, made it clear at the legislative level that the PP government would be relentless with fraud, evasion and tax evasion. The results in the fight against fraud support this statement. The Public Treasury has raised €44 billion in what we have been in the legislature, 5 billion more in six months less than in the previous socialist legislature, according to the latest known data. Fourthly, efforts were made to create an enabling environment for growth and job creation.
